## Inbox quality, entity logos, capture UX & onboarding walkthroughs (Rodrigo — 2026-08-30)

Five items Rodrigo raised after living with the merged sync engine for a day. Numbered in the
order he wants them tackled.

| ID | Item | Notes | Status |
|----|------|-------|--------|
| G60 | **Conflict resolution, Claude-Code style — time-aware, deduplicated, and "none of these" is an answer** (bug: two open "where does Rodrigo work" conflicts offering `mongodb` vs `supahost` when neither is current) | Today's conflict cards are wrong in four ways. **(1) No dedup:** `inbox_generator.py` never checks for an open conflict on the same entity+predicate, so every Sleep re-writes the same question (`_conflict_nudge` in `claim_reconciler.py`, `resolve_and_prune` in `conflict_resolver.py`). **(2) Time is ignored:** the item carries only `created_date` — not when each competing claim was last observed — even though the thesis says absence of mention is a signal; a `works_at` claim silent for 4 months should be *presented* as stale, and a fresh hint in conversation should refresh/close the conflict, not spawn a third. **(3) The answer set is closed:** option A / option B / "both are true" — no "neither, here's what's true now", no free text, no "ask me later". **(4) Resolve is lossy:** `_resolve_conflict` feeds the button label verbatim into an LLM body rewrite and ignores `claim_id`/`existing_claim_id`, so no claim is superseded/closed. **Design (mirrors Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion`):** every conflict/clarification item carries a *question object* — `question`, `options: [{label, description, claim_id, observed_at, last_referenced, age_days}]`, `allow_other: true`, `allow_defer: true` — with the question text and per-option descriptions written by the model at generation time (template fallback), including the age ("last mentioned 4 months ago"). App renders like `AskUserQuestion`: option rows with descriptions + a free-text "Other…" row + "Not sure / remind me later"; the model may also ask a short follow-up in-thread. Resolve semantics: picking an option **supersedes** the losing claim (bi-temporal close, `valid_to`), "both" keeps both with a `context` qualifier, free-text writes a `user_stated` claim (highest trust) and closes both, defer bumps `remind_after`. Dedup key `(entity_id, predicate)` with **merge-on-collision** (a new competing value joins the open item as another option instead of a new file). Time-aware refresh: Stage 3 re-scores open conflicts each Sleep — an option's claim re-mentioned since the item was created gets its `last_referenced` bumped and surfaces first; an open conflict whose entity/predicate the user *organically* clarifies in conversation is auto-resolved (path 1 of the three resolution paths). Same question-object shape extends to `clarification` and `merge_suggestion` kinds so the card UI is one component. → fixes the Inbox MVP §2/§3, relates to A3/R2 (claim layer), G53, temporal decay, and the `cicada_check_nudges` MCP surface (agent-initiated path gets the same object). **Shipped 2026-08-30:** question objects (`inbox_questions.py`), `(entity, predicate)` dedup with merge-on-collision (`inbox_generator.find_open`), `refresh_open_questions` in Stage 5.56, claim-aware resolve + defer (`inbox_service`), `QuestionView`/`QuestionSelection` in the app, `cicada_resolve_inbox` on MCP. | ✅ |
| G61 | **Update sources for facts — "where to look this up" as a first-class reference on entities & claims** | A contributor (model *or* user) can attach **sources** to a fact: a URL, a file, or a plain-English instruction ("my LinkedIn profile has my current job", "the `about` page of the company site lists the team"). Stored as a `sources:` frontmatter list on the entity (`{ref, kind: url|path|note, predicate?, added_by, added_at}`) and optionally on individual claims — distinct from `source_episodes` (provenance of *where a belief came from*) and from the `## Links` body section (loose bookmarks): a source is a **cheat-sheet for refreshing** a specific fact. Uses: (a) Stage 3 conflict scoring consults sources before asking — if a `works_at` conflict has a LinkedIn source, the model can propose "your profile says X" as the leading option, or resolve silently when trust rules allow; (b) the app's entity page shows sources with an "add source" row (URL or free text) and a "check now" action (`POST /entities/{id}/sources/check` → fetch → LLM compare → claim proposal via the inbox, never a silent overwrite); (c) `cicada_write_claim` accepts `sources=[…]` so a model can record "user told me to check <url> for this" mid-conversation. Fetching is keyless and rate-limited; private pages simply stay `note`-kind reminders the user acts on. → extends G60 (conflict inputs), M3/A2 (contributors), G9 (origins), G22/G30 (media & links). **Shipped 2026-08-30 (minimal slice):** `sources:` frontmatter via `api/services/fact_sources.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /entities/{id}/sources`, `cicada_write_claim(sources=)`, the conflict-card `hint`, and the app's entity Sources section. Fetching/"check now" remains a follow-up. | ✅ |
| G59 | **Entity logos — Revolut-style circular avatar next to the name** | Detect a logo for entities that plausibly have one (`company`, `tool`, some `project`/`media`) and render it as a circle beside the name in the detail card, inbox cards, search results and (optionally) as a canvas image on graph nodes. **Resolution ladder, keyless:** explicit `logo:` frontmatter → domain from the entity's `sources:`/`## Links`/`media.url` → domain guess from the name (LLM-free heuristics first: `mongodb` → `mongodb.com`; LLM only at Sleep for ambiguous names, recorded as a `website` claim) → favicon/touch-icon fetch (site's `apple-touch-icon` / `link rel=icon`, DuckDuckGo icon service as fallback) → cached PNG under `~/.cicada/logos/<entity-id>.png` (never in a bank) served by `GET /entities/{id}/logo` (ETag, 404 = monogram fallback in the app). Refreshed lazily (30-day TTL) so an updated brand mark shows up. App: `LogoImage` grows a remote/monogram mode (initials on the entity-type color when there is no logo, same size), avatar in `EntityDetailCard` header and `InboxCardView` title row. → relates to G58 (cached logos), G22 (media thumbnails), G61 (domain from sources). Shipped: api/services/logo_service.py (ladder + keyless fetch + ~/.cicada/logos/<bank>/ cache, 30 d hits / 7 d misses), GET /entities/{id}/logo, has_logo on graph nodes, a warm_logos Sleep tail step, LogoImage's entity mode (circle + monogram fallback) in the detail card / inbox / clusters / Ask chips, and an off-by-default "Show logos" graph toggle. | ✅ |
| G62 | **Capture page — show only what's connected, "+" to add (Revolut-style)** | The Capture page (`SourcesView.swift`, 1,341 lines) explains every channel up front: Import tiles, an RSS section with sample URL, a Calendars section with sample URL, Synced apps prose, origins strip, queue. Redesign: a single **"Connected"** list of the channels that actually have state (subscribed feeds, subscribed calendars, bookmark sync last run, Notes sync last run, Telegram if configured, chat-export imports by harness) as compact rows with a status line ("3 feeds · polled 2 h ago") and a row-level ⋯ menu (poll now, remove), plus one **`+` button** that opens a picker sheet of everything addable (Chat export, Bookmarks file, RSS feed, Calendar, Paste URL, Chrome/Safari bookmarks, Apple Notes, Telegram) — each with a one-line description *inside the sheet*, not on the page. Empty state = the queue card + a single "+ Add a source" call to action. Needs a small backend addition: `GET /sources/channels` (or fold into `/sources`) reporting per-channel connected state + last-sync timestamps so the page stops inferring connection from transient button results. → relates to G30, G29 (Telegram), M7, G9 (origin pills stay, but under the list). Shipped: GET /sources/channels derived from persisted state only (registries, sync_state.json, origin counts, the Telegram env flag), a channels sync domain, a Capture page that lists only connected channels with per-channel ⋯ actions, and an AddSourceSheet ("+", ⌘N) that owns all the explanatory copy. SourcesView is 1,341 → under 450 lines. | ✅ |
| G63 | **Connections page clarity — what "Claude plan · Connected" means, and Connect vs Connections** | Rodrigo: "I don't get how Claude is connected." | G70 | **Design memory — screenshots of design saves + a distilled design-identity layer** | Saved design inspiration (Pinterest boards, IG collections, bookmarked sites) should be *visual* memory: capture a screenshot per design-tagged media URL and distill a queryable design identity. **Capture (no screenshot feature exists in api/ or app/ today — verified by grep):** a Sleep tail step (pattern: `warm_logos`) shells headless system Chrome (`--headless --screenshot --window-size=1440,2400 <url>`), semaphore-bounded, offline-safe (failure = retry next cycle); login-walled pages = explicit opt-in capture via the user's own browser (Claude-in-Chrome/CDP), on-demand only — `screencapture -v` is the wrong tool (screen-not-page, TCC). **Storage — never in a bank**, same argument as logos (a shot of someone else's site is a derived artifact of the outside world): `~/.cicada/screens/<bank>/<url_hash>.png` + `meta.json`, ~30 d TTL, `GET /entities/{id}/screenshot` (ETag), `has_screenshot` on graph nodes read from the index only; user annotations DO enter the bank as body text/claims. **Design identity, zero schema change:** (i) Sleep-time VLM pass (G49 ladder; keyless degrade = skip) writes `palette`/`typography`/`layout-style` claims on each media entity — unseen predicates default multi-valued (verified, `predicates.py:171-186`), no seed edit; (ii) `exemplifies-style` claims with **node objects** → style `concept` entities ("brutalist web") project into real graph edges at Stage 5.7 (verified: `regenerate_edges_from_claims` projects only `object_kind == 'node'`), clustering the inspiration corpus in d3; (iii) the identity itself = a `skill` entity ("Design taste: …") distilled by **Stage 4 pattern detection** across design-tagged saves — its literal job, new input. **App:** the screenshot becomes the `HeroPreview`/`MediaPreview` hero for `url`-kind design media (preferred over the OG card; the app's `image` kind currently has **no server-side writer**), palette swatches under the G24 SummaryBox. **Not verified:** headless-Chrome flags against current stable Chrome; VLM model choice + palette-extraction quality (a pure-pixel k-means fallback needs no LLM at all); whether cookie-consent walls poison shots (they will — reuse/extend G65(b) junk detection at capture). **Prereq: G65(a)** slug collisions. → extends **G22** (media previews), **G23/G25** (hero), **G59** (derived-artifact cache pattern), **G47/G69** (the saves being screenshotted), **Stage 4** (skill distillation), **G37** later (capture at share time). **(Absorbs G14, merged 2026-09-01)** — the original aesthetics/mood-board ask: a first-class **`aesthetic`/mood-board** entity for designs/references that recur across projects, rich interlinking where an aesthetic *influences* projects (Rodrigo's example: **Blade Runner → futuristic cyberpunk → a robot-design project**), and a **dedicated gallery/board view** (not the force graph) for self-made designs cross-linked to Pinterest boards / external refs. A new `aesthetic` entity type was G14's original proposal; the "resist adding types" doctrine that closed **G2** argues against minting one — model cross-project influence via `exemplifies-style` claims on existing `concept`/`skill` entities instead, consistent with this row's own design (ii)/(iii). Watch-Later browser read + walkthrough recordings stay out of the slice. | ✅ shipped 2026-08-31 — Telegram save-with-reason + `saved-because` claim; staging-free `?preview=true` with per-collection counts; LinkedIn/TikTok/Reddit-GDPR export parsers; Pinterest + Reddit + X (Twitter, PKCE, pay-per-use cost honesty) connectors behind one shared `ADAPTERS` registry, `run_sync` driver, and a generalized `/sources/connectors/{id}/callback` OAuth route (channel rows, per-channel failure surfacing, Sleep-tail poll); Imports catalog with route badges and brand logo tiles; export overlay with breadcrumb step paths and live preview→confirm→dedup summary; guided connect flows via `ConnectorSetupPanel`. **2026-09-02 follow-up (`feat/safari-import`):** the catalog is logo-first and two-level — family tiles (Browsers, Websites & apps, Chat exports, Feeds & calendars, Files) → member tiles with their own mark, route lines and live channel state; arrows/Enter/Esc; Safari and Chrome marks are drawn in-app (`BrandGlyph`), official PNGs can be dropped into `Resources/logos/` later. **Partial/deferred:** walkthrough VIDEOS are still absent (`Resources/walkthroughs/` doesn't exist — every vendor renders the "coming soon" placeholder; G64); non-Takeout export ZIPs are not walked, so IG/TikTok/LinkedIn/Reddit archives must be unzipped first; Pinterest scope strings and Trial-tier read-of-existing-boards are still **unverified against the live API** (the official scopes page 404'd during research), as are Reddit's rate limits (secondary sources) and X's pricing, which the connector's own code flags as having churned 3x during 2026; TikTok oEmbed title fill is not attempted, so TikTok items land with slug titles; the Reddit GDPR export is not hydrated via `/api/info` (the existing OpenGraph enrichment covers it online and degrades to the permalink slug offline). YouTube Watch Later browser read and Google Data Portability stay out of scope, as specified. **Platform research archive (absorbed from G69, merged 2026-09-01) — the ground truth this shipped feature was built against, not otherwise repeated above:** the personal saved indexes Cicada wants are mostly **not API-reachable** — IG saved has no API at all (Basic Display dead Dec 2024; Graph API is business-only, no saved endpoint), YT watch-history/Watch-Later left the Data API in Sept 2016, LinkedIn has no consumer API and its ToS §8.2 bans scraping — so **aggregators cannot help**: Composio's own IG/YT/LinkedIn toolkits confirm zero saved/history actions, Zernio is publishing-only, and every hosted aggregator (Pipedream/Nango-hosted/Klavis/ACI) proxies tokens + content through its cloud, violating local-first. The optional keyed upgrade for YT history is Google's **Data Portability API** (`dataportability.myactivity.youtube`, 30/180-day grants + time filters = incremental), needing its own Google OAuth app — not pursued. Reddit's direct API is `GET /user/{name}/saved` (`history` scope, free non-commercial, **100 QPM, ~1k-item listing cap**), which is why the GDPR export backfills past the cap. If auth plumbing is ever wanted for a future connector: **Nango free self-host** (ELv2, tokens on-device) is the fallback — never a hosted proxy. |

Surface: a Prompts filter on the Feed/Ask, and `cicada_recall` can retrieve past prompts as reusable templates. The founding example is archived below in "Reference prompts". | 🔲 |
| G72 | **Agent-skills manager page + paper-guided skill lifecycle** (WikiSkill, Tang et al., Google Research, 2026-08-27 — [arXiv:2608.27454](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27454)) | Rodrigo's global agent skills are scattered, duplicated and silently drifting across three roots — **verified 2026-08-31 by read-only listing:** `~/.claude/skills/` = 3 personal (`context7-mcp`, `macos-harness`, `langfuse` — the last a **symlink out to `~/Developer/langfuse-skills/`**), `~/.claude/plugins/cache/*/skills/` = ~61 live across 7 installed plugins **plus 14 stale files from superpowers 6.2.0 still on disk beside 6.3.0**, and `~/.codex/skills/` = **92** dirs — of which **41 collide by exact name with the compound-engineering plugin's 41, and the copies have drifted** (`ce-plan/SKILL.md` differs byte-wise between the two roots); `~/.codex/prompts/` holds a third `ce-*` copy. Meanwhile `macos-harness`/`browser-harness`/`qa` (the heavily-used ones) exist in **one harness only**. **Slice A — inventory page, key-free, no LLM, buildable now:** a `skill_inventory` service walking the three roots (+ `installed_plugins.json` for marketplace/version/gitCommitSha provenance), a new **Skills** page under the G68 sidebar showing inventory by root, per-skill provenance and mtime, a **overlap/bloat panel** (name collisions, stale plugin versions, symlinks escaping the harness root, never-modified-since-install), and **per-harness sync state** (claude-only / codex-only / identical / drifted) with a diff — reuse the `DiffView` landing in **G67**, and the `/graph`-style ETag so a 156-entry walk costs a 304. **Slice B — Sleep-side candidate detection:** a Stage-4 tail step hashes/diffs same-named `SKILL.md`s and n-gram-overlaps distinct ones, emitting **existing inbox kinds** (`merge_suggestion` for near-dupes keyed on the sorted pair; a decay-flavoured question for a superseded plugin version or a skill untouched since install) — no new discriminator, no new resolve path. **Slice C — represent skills as memory:** each discovered skill → a `skill` entity (closed 10-set, no schema change) related to a `directory` entity for its harness root, its on-disk path as a `path`-kind **G61 `sources:`** ref; the paper's `PURPOSE.md` (skill → motivating evidence) is already Cicada's `source_episodes` + wikilinks. **What the paper actually gives us:** three-layer separation (`raw/` immutable traces → `wiki/` compounding pattern pages, *never rolled back* → `skills/` reversible), which maps 1:1 onto episodes → Stage-4 patterns → `skill` entities; and the ablation that **persistent knowledge is the whole gain** (Skill Proposer with wiki access 48.7 → 63.7 avg, +15.0) — i.e. Cicada's consolidation layer is the part worth having, not the optimizer. **Explicitly speculative / NOT committed:** the WikiSkill evolution loop itself. Its acceptance rule is `R(val) > R_best` on a labelled validation split, and a *personal* memory system has **no `D_val`** — there is no ground-truth score for "did this skill make the session better." Also missing: **`skill-impact.md`'s equivalent — nothing on the machine records which skill fired on which task with a pass/fail outcome** (verified: `~/.cicada/telemetry/` logs model+cost per LLM call, not skill invocation; Claude Code transcripts and `~/.codex/logs_2.sqlite` (38 MB) are unparsed). So any Cicada loop substitutes a **human gate**, which is exactly **R2**'s already-adopted governance (failure ledger + bounded, user-confirmed rewrites) and **G55**'s "skills never self-edit" — the paper *supports* R2: it accepts only ~1 in 3 proposed skill creations and ~1 in 4 edits (Table 4). **Two findings that constrain the design:** (i) **negative transfer is real** — a 4B-evolved skill dropped Gemini-3.5-Flash on SpreadSheet 50.5% → 18.1% by encoding harness-specific workarounds, so "push `macos-harness` to Codex" must be a **proposal with a diff, never an auto-push**; (ii) giving the *executing* agent wiki access **degraded** final skill quality (63.7 → 60.9), which cuts against Cicada's instinct to surface everything via `cicada_recall` — flag as an open question, not a decision. **Not verified:** whether Codex resolves `~/.codex/skills/` the same way Claude Code resolves `~/.claude/skills/` (assumed from directory shape + `SKILL.md` presence, not from docs); whether the 41 drifted copies are user edits or version skew; whether writing into harness roots from Cicada is safe (**Slices A/B are read-only — write-back is a separate, later decision**). → extends **G55** (executable skills / R2 governance — this is its inventory + provenance prerequisite), **A4** (procedural-memory capture), **R2**; consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G67** (DiffView), **G68** (sidebar slot), **Stage 4** (pattern detection, new input); relates to **G49** (skills shipped with the plugin). **Owner, 2026-09-03:** "a skills page — tied into the WikiSkill paper. There are skills I use a lot in Claude Code, for example the browser harness or the macOS harness; I'd like these documented in my skills too, and in that page see which skills we use the most." So the page has two halves: (a) **skills Cicada compiled about you** (G112 — the `skill` entities, each with its evidence, exportable as a SKILL.md bundle); (b) **skills you use in your harnesses** — read the *names and descriptions* of `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and the plugin skills the harness exposes, plus the harness's own usage counters (Claude Code keeps `skillUsage` in `~/.claude.json`: count + last used — the same class of harness-owned data the Activity page already reads from `~/.claude/stats-cache.json`; transcripts stay unread, G48), ranked by use; a used-a-lot external skill can be *adopted* into memory as a `skill` entity with a `uses` claim and the harness as observer, so Cicada's wiki knows your toolset the way it knows your projects. Rails: names/descriptions/counts only; nothing executes a skill; the page never edits `~/.claude`. | 🔲 |
| G74 | **Sleep has no engine — agent-driven consolidation** (Rodrigo 2026-08-31: "sleep is lowkey bs… it has never run because I have no credits; can the same agent contribute to the memory?") | **Verified:** the live bank's last real `Sleep cycle` commits are 2026-06-18 — every episode since (imports + today's captures) sits `processed: false`; the LLM stages silently no-op without an engine while the structural steps (migrations, warm_logos, refresh_open_questions) still run. Two concrete engines, both credit-free: **(a) the connected plan (fastest)** — implement G49's reserved `llm_mode="agent"` rung as `claude -p` subprocess calls through the user's already-connected Claude Max (G50 card shows Connected; compliance verified in [`subscription-first-portability.md`](subscription-first-portability.md): official scripted-use mode, token never touched, throttle-detect + stop), so the nightly Sleep just works; **(b) the in-session agent (the user's framing)** — the agent already talking to Cicada over MCP does consolidation itself: a `cicada_consolidate` tool (or librarian skill) hands the agent batches of unprocessed episodes + the extraction contract, the agent returns structured entities/claims through the existing trust-gated `write_claim`/staging paths, `Cicada-Author` records the actual model, G48 stamps the session — the 2026-07-14 "988 episodes → 442 claims" librarian run already proved this path with zero keys. Both rungs record to the G51 ledger (fixing the all-zeros dashboard, which was itself evidence Sleep never ran). Guard rails: engine choice surfaced honestly in the Sleep page ("no engine — connect a plan or run an agent consolidation"), never a silent no-op again — the current silence is the bug. **(Absorbs G80, merged 2026-09-01 — "the deterministic rung — keep the graph growing when no model is available"):** the argument, stated precisely so it isn't misread as "regex beats LLMs" — regex cannot resolve "Mongo"/"MongoDB"/"the database we switched to" (Stage 2's irreducibly semantic job); what a deterministic rung wins on is three **operational** properties: always **available**, **deterministic**, and **cannot hallucinate into versioned memory**. **The key mapping onto the north star:** the *curation* stream (a save, a bookmark, a paper, a `saved-because` reason) is largely pre-structured — the user already did the selecting — while the *experience* stream (chat prose, meetings) is not; so a deterministic rung keeps curation alive with no engine and cannot keep experience-extraction alive, which is exactly the shape of the 2.5-month outage: nothing was lost (`processed: false` is durable), but the product was dead. **Evidence this is cheap, not a rewrite (verified 2026-09-01 by grep): Sleep has 11 stages and only 5 call a model** — Stage 1 extraction, Stage 2 disambiguation, Stage 3 conflict/synthesis, Stage 4 skills, Stage 5.57 link enrichment. The other six already run today with no engine and zero LLM refs: Stage 5 nudge/inbox generation (`inbox_questions.py`), Stage 5.5 wikilink→`mentions` edges, Stage 5.55 media edges, Stage 5.56 the claim layer (`claim_reconciler.py`), Stage 5.6 hub tier + `_index.md`, Stage 5.7 `graph_builder.regenerate_edges_from_claims`; `promote_targets.py` and `vector_index.py` are likewise model-free, and the default embedding backend is on-device (EmbeddingGemma) — semantic clustering is already available with zero engine and zero cost. **What to build (exploration, then a slice):** a **Tier-0 structural write path** running whether or not an engine exists — harvest structure the user already supplied (URLs, domains, page titles, repo/file paths, hashtags, explicit `[[wikilinks]]`, Telegram `saved-because` reasons, platform collection/board names from G71, calendar attendees), recurrence counting against the promotion thresholds, embedding-based near-duplicate candidate generation, and structural decay/versioning (already works). **Tier-1** (still model-free): normalization + fuzzy match proposing `merge_suggestion` inbox items rather than performing them. **Tier-2** stays the LLM's: typed extraction, disambiguation, contradiction detection, pattern distillation. **The honest tension:** consolidation *is* the thesis claim — decay, versioned beliefs and per-belief provenance need a written structured graph, so Cicada cannot adopt zero-LLM writes wholesale without becoming RAG with extra steps; what it should adopt is the discipline that the graph's growth must not depend entirely on a component that can be unavailable. **Open questions:** does a Tier-0 entity earn a distinct `source_trust`/confidence band; does a later LLM pass upgrade a Tier-0 entity in place or supersede it as a claim; should Tier-0 run at capture time rather than only inside Sleep. | 🔲 |
| G75 | **Connection handshake — Cicada teaches every agent how to use it at connect time** (Rodrigo 2026-08-31: "for any agent to understand how to interact with cicada must be part as some sort of handshake") | The MCP `initialize` response carries a standard `instructions` field — today Cicada returns none (G48 only captures the INBOUND `clientInfo`). Build the outbound half: on initialize, return a compact, generated primer — what Cicada is, the interaction contract (check nudges at conversation start, save episodes as you learn things, `cicada_write_claim` with `sources:` for looked-up facts, ask-before-assuming on pending clarifications), the active bank + top-of-mind context (the **G53 state dictionary** is the natural payload: current projects, pending inbox count, recent conversations), and per-capability notes (resume availability, decay classes). Cached + cheap (no LLM; regenerated by structural Sleep / on write). Harnesses that ignore `instructions` get the same text via a `cicada_handshake` tool and the AGENTS.md/SessionStart-hook pointer (G49 plugin). This is also where per-agent expectations diverge (a Codex session vs Claude Code vs a raw client) — keyed off the captured `clientInfo`. → the runtime half of **G53**; extends **G48** (clientInfo), **G49** (primer hook); relates to G55/G72 (skills the handshake can advertise). **The inbox paragraph of the primer is fixed by G115:** after `cicada_recall`, call `cicada_check_nudges(entity_ids=<recall ids>)`; at most one question per turn, after the user's request is done; quote the Cause line; Recommended first; never a blocking question at the end of an unrelated turn; `skip=true` when unanswered and never re-ask that session; resolve only with the person's own answer; say what changed in one line; `normalization` items are app-only and the ask path never returns them. The FILTER is server-side and does not depend on the primer being read (`mode="ask"` gates on entity-id / vector-score match, the G98 gates, an in-process asked set and a 3-item limit); the primer carries only the discipline. Until this row and the SessionStart hook (G49/G76) land, the one seam that fires without the agent choosing is `cicada_recall`'s proactive inbox block, which G115 Phase 2 routes through the same gate. **Shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #45, `feat/state-handshake`):** `api/services/handshake.py` — ≤ 1,800 tokens, three variants (`claude-code`/`codex`/`generic`) over one contract, cached under `$CICADA_HOME/handshake/`; delivered as `initialize.instructions`, `cicada_handshake`, `GET /handshake`; recall hints carry a one-shot `state` block; `cicada_check_nudges` gained `entity_ids` so the discipline is executable; `handshake` ledger kind. **Final review (2026-09-03, `CONTRACT_VERSION` 2):** item 2 made honest against the tools — `cicada_resolve_inbox(id, skip=true)` exists as an in-process no-op (no write, not re-asked that MCP session), `cicada_check_nudges` filters `normalization` items, and the Cause line / `(Recommended)` marker are stated as "when the item shows them" (they are G115 Phase 1 card work). OPEN: the SessionStart hook / plugin (G49/G76 — `handshake.HOOK_POINTER` is the line it injects); per-harness variants beyond the three; G115 Phase 2's server-side gate; the Cause line and `(Recommended)` marker on the inbox files themselves (G115 Phase 1). | ✅ |

| G77 | **Voice-first Cicada + the "output packet" contract** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, forwarding [@KingBootoshi](https://x.com/KingBootoshi)'s voice-agent build: "add this as inspiration of things to discuss as a to-do") | **The idea worth stealing, restated:** a voice UX must be fast, fast means a small model, and a small model is not smart enough to compress an intelligent agent's work without losing the point. His fix inverts the usual one — **don't push intelligence down into the voice layer; make the intelligent layer emit a packet so plainly written that the small model cannot mistranslate it.** The voice agent becomes a reader/typist, not an interpreter, and the packet is the contract between them. **Why this lands squarely on Cicada:** Cicada already produces exactly the artifacts that would fill such packets — inbox questions (G60 already carry `question` + `options[{key,label,description}]` + `allow_other`/`allow_defer`, i.e. a *closed-choice* structure a dumb reader can voice verbatim and an ASR can map back by key), `/ask` answers with citations + gap analysis, Sleep-cycle summaries, decay nudges. The G60 question object IS a first draft of an output packet; the gap is that nothing renders one for *speech* (no plain-language rendering, no per-option pronounceable key, no "what changed since we last spoke" digest). **Sub-ideas to discuss, not committed:** (i) a `packet` projection of any inbox item / ask answer / cycle summary — short sentences, no jargon, explicit enumerated choices, a single question at the end; testable by round-trip (small model reads packet → user's spoken reply → key recovered without the big model in the loop); (ii) **the green light** — a presence/status surface saying "Cicada is awake / consolidating / has 3 things to ask you", the ambient version of the Sleep page; (iii) **speaker-verification as auth** — his neural net that recognizes only his voice, i.e. an *observer identity* signal; Cicada's claim layer already has `observer:` and `source_trust` (`user_stated` vs `mcp`), so a verified-voice channel is a trust tier, not a new schema; (iv) the FaceTime/daemon adapter pattern (answer calls only from one number) as a capture channel alongside Telegram (G62 channel registry + the G71 `ADAPTERS` seam is the shape); (v) delegation — voice states an intent, a slow smart agent (G74(b) librarian / a Hermes-style daemon) does the work, packet comes back; the `processed:false` queue is already the async hand-off contract. **Open questions:** does the packet belong in the bank as memory (it is derived — probably NOT, same argument as logos/screenshots), or is it purely a rendering? Does a spoken correction write a `user_stated` claim directly (it should — that is the highest-trust input Cicada can get)? What is the latency budget end-to-end before it stops feeling like a person? → extends **G60** (question objects = proto-packets), **G53** (state dictionary = the digest source), **G74(b)** (the slow-agent half of delegation), **G62** (a new capture channel), **G54** (the interview beat this would make voice-native); relates **G75** (the handshake is the text twin of "here is how to talk to me"). | 🔲 |
| G78 | **Re-evaluate gbrain (and its eval harness) against Cicada** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "search if gbrain's approach is actually better for what we try to do, or if there is something he does better that maybe we can learn from"; also [`gbrain-evals`](https://github.com/garrytan/gbrain-evals) "to see if any of that can apply for us") | Cicada already has a 2026-era analysis at [`../inspiration/gbrain.md`](../inspiration/gbrain.md) — "the convergent twin: markdown+git, self-wiring typed graph, overnight enrichment, MCP" — and the D3 ruling ("answer not pages") was taken partly from it. **This row is the re-check, and it has a new object:** `gbrain-evals` did not exist when that analysis was written. **Two deliverables.** **(1) Approach diff, honestly scored.** Re-read the current gbrain source (not the old notes) and answer, per axis, "who is actually better and why": zero-LLM-on-write (its regex wikilink cascade) vs Cicada's LLM extraction — gbrain's write path costs nothing and never hallucinates, which is *strictly better on the axis Cicada is currently dead on* (G74: no engine ⇒ no writes at all; a regex/structural promotion path would have kept the graph alive these 2.5 months, and that is an argument for a **zero-LLM fallback rung**, not just a cheaper engine); dedup/citation-repair as background chores; its `think` synthesis vs Cicada's `/ask`; typed-graph conventions vs the closed 10-type set; decay (Cicada's differentiator — verify gbrain still has no temporal-decay analogue); claims/bi-temporality (Cicada's other differentiator); provenance depth (Cicada's `Cicada-Author`/`Cicada-Session` trailers + git blame are, as far as the prior analysis found, unmatched). **(2) Eval harness applicability — the higher-value half.** Cicada's thesis needs a defensible Results chapter and today has `benchmarks/` with a **manual four-dimensional scoring rubric** (`sections/experiments.tex`) — i.e. a human scores every answer, which does not scale and is the weakest link in the thesis. If gbrain-evals provides task sets, an automatic judge, or a recall/precision protocol for a *personal markdown memory graph*, adopting or adapting it (with attribution) directly strengthens Table 1/2/3. Assess: what does it measure, on what corpus, with what judge; is the corpus synthetic (portable) or personal (not); can it run against Cicada's API without a rewrite; does it have anything for the two things Cicada claims and others don't (**decay behavior over simulated time**, and **provenance/attribution correctness**) — if not, that gap is itself a thesis contribution to state. **Method:** read-only clone/read of both repos, no dependency adoption in this row; output = a written comparison in `docs/inspiration/gbrain.md` (updated, dated) + concrete "adopt / adapt / reject, and why" rulings, each cross-linked to the row it would change. **Explicitly allowed conclusion:** "gbrain is better at X and we should copy X." → re-opens **D1** (storage backend) and **D3** (answer-not-pages, already decided); feeds **benchmarks/** + the thesis Results; relates **G74** (the zero-LLM fallback argument), **A5** (gap analysis, taken from this lineage). | 🔲 |
| G79 | **North star — Cicada is worth having only if it learns from lived experience AND curation, and gives it back at the right moment** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the best version of cicada is one that smartly gives and learns from all my experience as a human + the things that i've curated and might be relevant to me") | Not a feature — the sentence every other row is scored against, recorded so it can arbitrate scope. **Read literally it names two intake streams and one output discipline.** *Experience* = what actually happened to the user: conversations (MCP capture, imports), decisions and their reversals, work done in repos, places, people, the passage of time itself (the decay layer is the only part of Cicada that models experience as something that *fades*). *Curation* = what the user deliberately chose to keep: saves with reasons (G71), bookmarks, papers, design references (G70), prompts (G73), skills (G72) — signal the user paid attention to select, which is categorically different from conversational exhaust and should probably carry different trust and different decay (already true structurally: media/bookmarks are `evergreen`, G66). *Smartly gives* = the output discipline, and the part Cicada is furthest from: **relevance at the right moment, unprompted, without noise.** Today Cicada answers when asked (`/ask`, `cicada_recall`) and interrupts on a schedule (inbox); it does not yet notice "you are about to do X and three months ago you curated the exact thing that helps." **The measurable claim hiding in the sentence** (worth stating as a thesis-grade goal): the system should surface a *curated* item inside an *experiential* context the user never explicitly linked — i.e. cross-stream retrieval, not within-stream search. That is a testable capability (does a Pinterest save from March ever legitimately reach a September conversation about a project?) and today the honest answer is "only if the user searches for it." **Use as a filter:** a row that only adds intake (more capture channels) advances half the sentence; a row that only adds surfaces advances none of it; rows that connect curated artifacts into live context (G70's design identity, G60's timed questions, G75's handshake priming, G53's state dictionary, G52's Ask) advance the whole of it — score accordingly. **Tension to keep honest:** "gives smartly" and "never intrusive" (UX principle 4) pull against each other; the resolution so far has been pull-not-push, and if that stays fixed, the ceiling on "smartly gives" is set by whatever the agent loads at conversation start — which makes **G75** (handshake) and **G53** (state dictionary) load-bearing for the north star, not niceties. → the scoring lens over the whole table; most directly served by **G53**, **G75**, **G52**, **G70**, **G66**, **G71**; the reason **G74** is the top blocker (nothing learns while Sleep is dead). | 🔲 |
| G80 | *(merged into [G74](#g74) — the deterministic-rung argument, including the "11 Sleep stages, only 5 call a model" verified evidence and the Tier-0/1/2 proposal, now lives there)* | — | — |
| G81 | **Contacts connection — real identity anchors for `person` entities** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "allow connection with contacts (name/email/phone numbers and stuff)") | Today a `person` entity is a name and whatever a conversation said about them; there is no anchor tying "Aitor" in a chat to an actual human. Connecting the address book gives every person node a stable identity: **canonical name + email(s) + phone(s) + organization**, which unlocks (i) *dedup that actually works* — two pages for the same human collapse on a shared email rather than on fuzzy name similarity, the weakest input Stage 2 has; (ii) **G61 `sources:` for people** — an email or a LinkedIn URL is literally "where to look this fact up"; (iii) the **recorded-meetings** ambition now in `CLAUDE.md` — a transcript's speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to known humans, so this is a prerequisite, not a nicety; (iv) relationship inference the graph cannot currently make (same employer, same domain in an email address, a shared org). **Route:** macOS-native first — `CNContactStore` (Contacts framework) read-only, which is a **TCC-gated permission** the user grants once; note the app is currently unsigned (see the runtime row), and TCC prompts behave differently for an unsigned/ad-hoc-signed bundle — verify before promising a smooth grant. A one-shot vCard/CSV import is the keyless fallback and the portable path (Google Contacts export, iCloud vCard). Follow the **connector seam** that G71 established (`api/services/connectors/`, one adapter module, `ADAPTERS` registry, `base.run_sync`, `record_credentials_changed`) so this is one more adapter rather than a bespoke integration. **The hard part is not the API, it is the privacy rail — and it is different from every prior source.** Every other stream is the user's own data; a contact record is **a third party's PII** (someone else's phone number), landing in a **git-versioned, permanently-historied** store. Rails to decide before building: does the full record enter the bank at all, or only a minimal anchor (canonical name + a stable hash for matching) with the raw email/phone held outside the bank under `~/.cicada/` like logos and screenshots (the "derived/external artifact" precedent — although a contact is *source* data, not derived, so the precedent argues by analogy only)? Sensible default: **only what a fact needs** — an email used as a `sources:` ref is justified; a home address ingested "because it was in the record" is not. Also: contact records must never be swept wholesale into episodes (that would put an address book into the extraction queue); matching must be a **proposal** (`merge_suggestion` inbox kind, already exists) and never an automatic merge, because a wrong person-merge silently fuses two humans' histories — the most damaging error this graph can make. **Open questions:** whether to sync continuously (contacts change) or import once and re-check on demand; whether a contact with no conversational trace should create an entity at all (it would flood the graph with hundreds of inert people — probably NOT: a contact should *enrich* an existing person entity, not mint one); how deletion propagates when the user removes a contact. → prerequisite for the recorded-meetings half of the project goal; extends **G61** (`sources:` on people), **G71** (connector seam), **G60** (`merge_suggestion` as the proposal surface), **Stage 2** (identity resolution's best possible input); relates **G77** (voice identity/observer trust) and **G79** (curation stream). **(Absorbs G46, merged 2026-09-01)** — the original ask specifically wanted read-only AppleScript/EventKit enumeration of Contacts.app feeding entity resolution as corroborating metadata, not raw episodes, and covers most family-milestone birthdays. | 🔲 |
| G82 | **Hub pages are not addressable — the entity card half-works for them and only History admits it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, running dev: "nodes like architecture have a couldnt load history error") | **Root-caused, verified live.** `api/routers/entities.py:189-191` hardcodes `memory_path / "entities" / f"{id}.md"` and 404s **before git is ever called**. Hub pages don't live there — they're `hubs/topic-architecture.md`, and `graph_builder.py:215` mints their id as `hub:<stem>`, so `entities/hub:topic-architecture.md` can never exist. Confirmed by curl: `/entities/hub:topic-architecture/history` → 404 while `/entities/awake-sleep-architecture/history` → 200 with 2 entries; the data is fine (`git blame hubs/topic-architecture.md` = 141 attributed lines across 3 commits). **Why only History complains:** the other tabs swallow the identical 404 — `Store.swift:531-532` returns nil on throw so the header keeps rendering the graph-node stub, and `EntityDetailCard.swift:1355` uses `try? … ?? []` so Perspectives/Timeline render as merely empty. History is the one tab whose state machine separates error from empty, so it is the only honest surface. **Scope is systemic, not one line:** eleven sites in `entities.py` (61, 113, 189, 205, 230, 301, 375, 421, 481, 494, 519) and four in `git_service.py` (211, 352, 590, 898) hardcode the same wrong directory. `GET /entities/{id}`, `/history`, `/context` and `/claims` all 404 for hubs. **Both layers must move together:** deleting only the router guard flips the symptom to a false "no history", because `git_service.py:352` independently rebuilds `entities/{id}.md` and returns `[]`. **Fix:** one shared `resolve_entity_file(memory_path, id) -> Path | None` mapping a `hub:` prefix to `hubs/<stem>.md` and everything else to `entities/<id>.md`, plus a repo-relative twin for git; route every read-only site through it. Never special-case the string in the view. | 🔲 |
| G83 | **Buttons need several clicks and only respond on the text — 37 sites, one root cause** (Rodrigo: "the clusters back button sometimes i have to click several times… and i've noticed that problem with many buttons… Buttons should also be more interactive with their colors and stuff when i click so they feel snappy") | **Root cause, verified.** `TopicsView.swift:707-721`: the Button's label is a bare `HStack { Image; Text }` with only `.foregroundStyle` + `.padding`. With `.buttonStyle(.plain)` the label paints no background of its own, so the hit region is SwiftUI's default content shape — **the union of the children's rendered glyphs**. Padding adds layout size but NOT hit area, which is exactly why clicking the chevron or the word works and the surrounding pill does not. The visible pill comes from `.glassCard(...)` applied *after* `.buttonStyle` — i.e. to the Button, painted as a sibling layer behind it, never part of the tap target. **Scope: 37 of 87 `.buttonStyle(.plain)` sites have neither `.contentShape` nor a background inside the label; only 7 use `.contentShape` today.** **Fix:** one shared `ButtonStyle` in `CicadaTheme` whose `makeBody` wraps `configuration.label` with `.contentShape(Rectangle())` — because the style wraps the label, a single definition repairs every adopting site at once — and give it pressed-state styling (scale/opacity/background shift keyed on `configuration.isPressed`) so the snappiness lands in the same change. The per-site one-liner (`.contentShape(Rectangle())` inside the label closure) is the stopgap for anything the shared style can't reach. | ✅ shipped 2026-09-01 (PR #23) — `CicadaPlainButtonStyle`/`CicadaGlassButtonStyle` in `app/CicadaApp/Sources/CicadaApp/Theme/CicadaTheme.swift:395-462` add `.contentShape(Rectangle())` + pressed-state scale/opacity to the shared button styles. |
| G84 | **Graph: cold first paint hides nodes, drags stop dead, and the legend describes a different axis than the colors** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, four graph symptoms) | Four separate causes, all verified. **(a) Missing nodes on first entry** — `Coordinator.pushGraphData()` (`GraphView.swift:164-171`) pushes the full snapshot but never follows with `applyFilters`, unlike the other push site (`:74-79`, whose comment literally says "defaults hide … from first paint"). So the cold paint runs on graph.js's own defaults: `filters.minDegree = 1` (`graph.js:154`) drops every zero-degree node at `:702`, while Swift's default is `minDegree = 0` (`GraphFilter.swift:11`). The first control the user touches makes `prevMinDegree 1 ≠ 0`, `rebuildVisible()` runs, and the missing nodes appear — and stay. **Fix:** push the filter in `pushGraphData`'s completion handler exactly as `:76-78` does, and make the two defaults agree. **(b) Drag stops instantly** — not a stuck pin: `onMouseUp` (`graph.js:1386-1407`) correctly nulls `fx/fy` and zeroes `alphaTarget`. It's structural — while `fx/fy` are set d3 zeroes the node's velocity every tick, so at release `vx/vy` are exactly 0 and a throw is impossible by construction; pointer velocity is never sampled in `onMouseMove`. Compounded by `velocityDecay(0.55)` (`:770`, d3's default is 0.4 → motion damps in ~4 ticks/~65 ms). **Fix:** record the per-move world-space delta during drag, seed `vx/vy` from it on release before unpinning, then `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2)).restart()`. Related latent bug in the same seam: mouseup binds only on the canvas (`:1299-1302`), so releasing outside it never unpins. **(c) Legend vs colors** — two different fields conflated: node fill comes from `n.type` for all 1,769 non-facet nodes (`graph.js:1003-1005`), while the legend keys off the **claim-context** axis (`contextRoster`, `GraphViewModel.swift:180-183`, derived from valid claims at `graph_builder.py:141-153`). Only 89 of 1,858 nodes are facets. Worse, the palettes deliberately collide — `family → #F65BA6` is byte-identical to `typeColors.media`, `engineering → #2DD4BF` = tool, `career → #FF8A3D` = company — which is exactly why selecting "family" appears to highlight bookmarks. **Fix:** give contexts a palette disjoint from `typeColors` (duplicated in `graph.js:63-70` AND `CicadaTheme.swift:211-218` — change both or they desync), or stop encoding context as fill entirely and carry it on edges + facet rings, which is what actually holds context data; and label the legend for what it describes. **(d) All/Cicada/You/External means nothing** — it filters on **claim observer**, dim-only (alpha 0.1, `graph.js:1013`), and returns true unconditionally for the 56% of nodes with no claims (`:269-274`), so three segments look identical. Honest meanings, with live counts: All = no lens; Cicada = pages where the agent asserted a currently-valid claim (740); You = pages where you personally asserted one (107); External = pages where someone else is on record (3). **Fix:** relabel to `Everything · Inferred by Cicada · Stated by you · Heard from others`, and gate segments on non-empty membership the way `hasObserverDiversity` already gates the whole bar. **(a)(b) shipped 2026-09-01, PR #23** — `GraphView.swift:164-171` `pushGraphData` now re-applies filters on cold paint (a); `graph.js` throw-velocity tracking (`onMouseMove`/`onMouseUp`, `velocityDecay(0.45)`) fixes the drag-stop (b), both marked `G84a`/`G84b` in code comments. **(c)(d) remain open** — see TODO Wave B. | 🛠️ (a)(b) shipped; (c)(d) open |
| G85 | **Decay is attributed to a model, re-charges the same interval every run, never strengthens, and its rate ignores how established a belief is** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "i see decay something changed by the model, and i belive it should be not something of the model itself but as time passes by… its decay depends on how established the fact is and it can go from evergreen ad very established to something that isnt, and vice-versa") | **The user is right, and the diagnosis splits into four distinct defects.** **(1) Attribution is wrong, the math is not.** The decay loop (`conflict_resolver.py:128-181`) is pure arithmetic over frontmatter and `datetime.now()` — `decay_amount = rate * days_since/7`, no LLM anywhere. But authorship is decided **once per commit for the whole batch**: `sleep_cycle.py:798-804` builds `authors` from `settings.litellm_model` regardless of any change's trigger, and `git_service.py:399-400` applies that commit-level trailer to every entity line. So a deterministic time computation is stamped with whichever model happened to run that cycle — 978 such lines across the two 2026-06-18 cycles, which also inflates that model's counts in `GET /contributors`. **Fix:** split `trigger: sleep/decay` changes into their own commit authored `Cicada-Author: cicada` — the literal already reserved for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop. **(2) Decay is not actually continuous in time — it re-charges.** `conflict_resolver.py:145-151` SUBTRACTS `rate * days_since/7` from stored confidence but the write branch (`:336-341`) never advances `last_referenced` and stamps no watermark, so the next run reads the same `last_referenced` and subtracts the same full interval again. **Confidence is a function of how many times Sleep ran, not of elapsed time** — this is the concrete defect behind the objection. Same shape in the claim engine (`claim_reconciler.py:478-483`). **Fix:** make it computed, not accumulated — keep a stable `baseline_confidence` and derive `confidence = baseline * exp(-rate * weeks_since_last_referenced)`; or stamp `decayed_through:` and measure from `max(last_referenced, decayed_through)` so each interval is charged exactly once. **(3) Rate ignores establishedness.** `decay_class` is assigned once at page creation (`:211-222`) and never revised — the update branch merges episodes, tags, aliases and body but never touches it. An entity referenced in 50 conversations decays at the rate it was born with. `source_episodes` is the natural establishedness signal and is already accumulated. **Fix:** in the update branch, promote the class one step toward `durable` when the page crosses a distinct-conversation threshold; keep `evergreen` off-limits to the agent per the existing `agent_class` rail. **(4) Bidirectionality is half-built.** Recovery exists (`:248-258`) but is a one-shot floor — `max(current, 0.6)` — gated on LLM re-extraction, so a belief reinforced ten times still lands on 0.6 and can never climb. **Fix:** make reinforcement a bounded additive increment symmetric with decay, both directions reading the same `decay_policy` resolver. → this is the row that makes "time as a signal" honest; touches **G66** (decay classes), **G80** (all four fixes are deterministic — no engine needed). | 🔲 |
| G86 | **Feed is an undifferentiated list, ~22% of it duplicate rows** (Rodrigo: "relevance and recent here seem to have the same view, and the view all in all i think it can be improved, its just a huge list of bookmarks, maybe something easier to navigate different types would be better") | **Verified and worse than it looks: 789 rows render 603 distinct pages.** `media_ingestor` slugifies the page TITLE into `media_entity_id`, so **148 separate Google consent-interstitial bookmarks collapse onto one entity** (`media-before-you-continue-to-google-search`, plus 27 on the Portuguese variant), while `GET /sources` iterates the url_index one row per URL (`sources.py:361`) — so all 175 render as visually identical rows with the same title, site and score. That padding is a large part of why the list feels unnavigable. **The Relevance-vs-Recent symptom reproduces but its first-pass cause was REFUTED on re-check — treat as unresolved and re-derive before fixing.** **Work:** (a) collapse rows sharing a `mediaEntityId` in any grouping UI, and stop minting media pages for consent/interstitial titles at ingest (prereq **G65(a)** slug collisions); (b) re-diagnose the sort toggle honestly; (c) grouping axes that exist on the wire today are thin — `MediaSourceItem` (`schemas.py:1082-1096`) has no `folder` and no `origin`, so navigation by platform/collection needs the payload widened first, which is the real prerequisite. **(Absorbs G65, merged 2026-09-01 — the fix-level detail from that row):** for (a), `media_ingestor._media_entity_id` slugifies the page *title* (not the URL), which is the root cause of the 148-way collision on "Before you continue to Google Search" — append the URL-hash suffix whenever the slug already exists for a *different* normalized URL (not only when >120 bytes), plus a one-shot migration for existing collisions. For (b), those 148 saves are Google consent interstitials, not content — detect by title/URL pattern at ingest, fetch through or mark `status: dropped`, and sweep existing ones. G65 also measured **why "Relevance" and "Recent" look identical**: the Feed badge shows §3.4 decayed confidence, constant ≈57% after a bulk sync because every item shares identical defaults and `personal_relevance_weight` is never written — either wire the search query into a vector-index score server-side or vary the inputs (per-item `last_referenced` bumps on open, weight from Sleep). *(G65's first-pass cause for the sort-toggle symptom was itself refuted on re-check — treat the toggle as genuinely unresolved, not just unmeasured, and re-derive before fixing.)* UI-side row-identity fixes (unique `entityId|url` key, badge hidden while rendered percentages are uniform) already shipped 2026-08-31. | 🔲 |
| G87 | **Bank selection: the invariant is already true, the product just never says so — and one import path can silently write to a non-active bank** (Rodrigo: "the view to select different projects or create new ones can be improved. We have to be able to select only one active which is the one we contribute to by default") | **Verified: exactly-one-active is ALREADY the model.** `banks.yaml` has a single scalar `active:`; `bank_registry.activate_bank:279` is a plain assignment; `resolve_active_bank_path:64-92` returns one directory; no multi-active representation exists anywhere. And **every automatic writer resolves it fresh** — `Settings.memory_path` is a computed property (`config.py:39-41`), the MCP server re-resolves per tool call (`mcp/server.py:480-500`), and Telegram, links, bookmarks, RSS, connector syncs, Sleep and inbox resolution all route through it. The user is asking for a guarantee the system already makes and never states. **The one real hole:** `POST /banks/{name}/import` (`banks.py:162-207`) writes into an explicitly named bank that need not be active and never activates it, and the app exposes it as a free picker (`UploadOverlay.swift:203-224`). Episodes staged into a non-active bank are invisible to Sleep — **a whole chat-history export can silently go nowhere**, and it is the highest-volume write in the product. **Fix:** on a non-active import, replace the success toast with the consequence plus a one-click remedy ("Imported into X — not your active project, so it won't be consolidated. [Switch to X]"), or drop the picker and always import into the active bank. **Two more gaps:** (i) the switcher's only discriminator is a raw `glob("*.md")` count (`bank_registry.py:213-244`), so **the biggest number belongs to the most abandoned bank** — add the bank's git HEAD commit date and render "last written today" / "never consolidated", plus `DELETE /banks/{name}` guarded against the active one; (ii) `BankSwitcher` is instantiated exactly once, inside the Graph tab's overlay (`ContentView.swift:197`), so **the active bank is invisible on the 5 other pages that write to it** — hoist it to the sidebar footer beside the Settings gear, which also fixes the bank-vs-filter confusion by physically separating it from the graph's lens chips (today both render with the identical `.glassCard` at the same font, unlabelled). **Latent, not yet biting:** the sleep schedule is loaded once from the BOOT bank (`main.py:119`) and never re-registered on activate, so after a switch the schedule comes from the old bank while the cycle consolidates the new one. | 🛠️ invariant half shipped (`315e077`, PR #24 — `BankImportResponse.active` + a one-click "Switch to X" toast when an import lands in a non-active bank). Remaining: the BankSwitcher discriminator (git HEAD date vs. raw glob count), hoisting the switcher out of the Graph tab into the sidebar footer, and the boot-bank sleep-schedule staleness bug are all still open. |
| G88 | **Run Cicada like an installed app, not two terminals** (Rodrigo: "super important is to have a way where i can easily run cicada all the time in my laptop as if i already downloaded the app… and as i develop it receives updates as it would if it where deployed") | **Half is already done as of 2026-09-01:** the backend runs under launchd (`~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cicada.backend.plist`, RunAtLoad+KeepAlive, `python -m uvicorn`) — and the installer bug that blocked it is fixed (a venv console script hardcodes its interpreter in the shebang, so moving the repo broke `api/.venv/bin/uvicorn` with `EX_CONFIG` and an empty log). **App bundling also already exists and was not being used:** `app/CicadaApp/bundle.sh` + `make app` / `make run-app` assemble a real `.app` (its own header explains why: `swift run` yields a bundle-less executable whose window never becomes key, which silently breaks graph node clicks and text-field focus — i.e. the current dev loop is actively worse than the packaged one). **Verified non-issue:** the app does NOT fight launchd for port 8000 — `BackendProcess.start()` probes the port first (`:21-25`) and returns before assigning `process`, so quitting the app also can't kill the launchd backend. CLAUDE.md's "spawns the backend" line should be corrected to "spawns only if nothing already holds :8000". **Two real gaps to close:** (i) **the always-on backend has no durable API keys** — the plist carries only `CICADA_MEMORY_PATH`/`PYTHONPATH`, `~/.cicada/secrets.env` (the one supported store, loaded at `main.py:75`) does not exist, and `api/.env` is never exported into `os.environ` where consumers actually read it (`providers.py:254`), so after a reboot embeddings silently degrade to local and LLM calls fail quietly; (ii) `BackendProcess.installRoot()` (`:97-104`) branches on `.build`/`DerivedData` in the bundle path, so an installed `~/Applications/Cicada.app` resolves to `~/cicada` — which *exists* on this machine and therefore resolves plausibly rather than failing loudly, feeding a wrong path into the Connect page's copy-paste MCP commands. **Fix:** stamp the checkout path into the generated Info.plist at bundle time and prefer it. **Remaining work:** a `make install-app` that builds `--release` and dittos into `~/Applications`, replace-while-running semantics, and an optional LaunchAgent for the app itself; unsigned-app Gatekeeper first-run is the honest trade-off to state. **Shipped 2026-09-01 (PR #28, merged to dev):** `make install-app` (release build → stage → verify → swap into `~/Applications`, previous bundle recoverable; an interrupted swap is recovered on the next run), `make dev` (debug rebuild + relaunch — the everyday loop), `make login-item` (System Events Login Item rather than a hand-rolled LaunchAgent — see `login_item.sh`), `installRoot()` demoted to a fallback: the Connect page now takes `memoryRoot` from `GET /healthz` whenever a backend answers (`LiveMemoryRootProbe`, backoff 0.5→8 s, never regresses to the guess), so the app and the agents can no longer point at two banks; every snippet path is escaped per format (`SnippetEscape`). README run section rewritten. Gatekeeper first-run for the unsigned bundle stays the stated trade-off. | ✅ |
| G89 | **Substack — following already works; make FEEDS first-class instead of building a connector** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "add to backlog an ability to connect substack if possible") | **Researched against primary sources and verified live; the headline is that a Substack connector is the wrong shape.** Subscribing to a publication is already supported end-to-end — `feed_registry.py` + `feeds.yaml`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`, `media_ingestor.parse_rss` → the same dedup/ingest pipeline as bookmarks — and the app's RSS tile literally reads *"A blog or Substack Cicada checks for new posts"* (`AddSourceSheet.swift:64`), with the backend docstring saying the same (`sources.py:216`). **What Substack actually exposes (verified 2026-09-01):** `https://<pub>.substack.com/feed` is documented and live; RSS 2.0, **full body for free posts** (an ACX item ran 57k+ chars in `content:encoded`), **exactly 20 items with no pagination** — a recent window, *not* an archive, so history cannot be backfilled from it; custom-domain publications serve `/feed` on their own domain and the `*.substack.com` form 301s to it. **Paywalled posts appear with full metadata but a ~142-byte stub body** containing only a "Read more" anchor — a reliable detection heuristic, and a real ingestion hazard today (that stub would land as a normal media entity with an empty body). **There is no authenticated text feed at all**: Substack's private per-subscriber RSS is **podcast-only**, so the sanctioned machine-readable route to paid text is *none*. **A reader's own subscription list has no sanctioned route either** — no OPML export (help-centre search for "OPML" returns 0 results), no reader data export (Exports are publisher-side, for a publication you own), and no documented API. The only mechanism is `GET substack.com/api/v1/subscriptions` with a session cookie: undocumented, cookie-gated, no stable contract — **the same unsanctioned shape already ruled out for the X archive and LinkedIn enrichment**, so it stays out. Defensible alternative: the user copies their list from `substack.com/library` by hand once, and each publication is subscribed via its public feed — a user action, not automated access. **Saved posts, likes and Notes have no export or API**; Substack's own MCP server states it "cannot access profile data or Notes activity". The official **Developer API is a creator-profile lookup keyed on a LinkedIn handle** (no post content, no reader data), and the Substack MCP server is publisher analytics gated on being an Admin of a *Bestseller* publication — neither is usable here. **So the work is feed quality, and it is all currently missing:** (i) **`parse_rss` keeps only 4 fields** — link, title, body, categories (`media_ingestor.py:993-998`) — and drops `pubDate`, `dc:creator`, `<enclosure>` and the channel `<title>`, so an ingested post has **no publish date, no author, and no publication**; (ii) `RawItem.origin` is never set for feed items, so an RSS post is indistinguishable from a pasted URL in `/origins` (contrast `"chrome-bookmark"`, `"tiktok-saved"`); (iii) **the publication is not an entity** — only individual posts, so "what does this writer keep saying" is unanswerable and G79's cross-stream retrieval has nothing to hang on; (iv) **feed polling is NOT on the Sleep tail** — `sleep_cycle._poll_connectors_safely` iterates only `connectors.ADAPTERS`, so feeds refresh only when a human presses Poll (calendars share this gap); (v) a feed record is just `{url, tags, added, last_polled}` — no title, no etag/last-modified (so every poll refetches in full), no per-feed error, no enabled flag, hence no failure surfacing on the channel row; (vi) no bulk/OPML import path for adding many feeds at once; (vii) paywall-stub items need skipping (or marking) rather than ingesting. **Also inconsistent:** feeds are gated **opt-in** (`CICADA_ALLOW_FEED_FETCH == "1"`) while connectors are now opt-out — one of the two should move. → extends **G47/G71** (the ingest family and the connector seam that feeds should join), **G61** (a publication is a natural `sources:` anchor), **G79** (a followed writer is curation, and the publication entity is what makes it relatable); relates **G80** (all of this is deterministic, zero-LLM work). | 🛠️ feed-following half already shipped (M4 — `feed_registry.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`); the metadata-quality work (i)-(vii) above is the open remainder — see TODO Wave D. |
| G90 | **README refresh — screenshots + current integrations** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "update the readme with new screenshots and updated info as to integrations and stuff… once the queue of tasks is done") | **Queued deliberately AFTER the G74(a) engine queue** — a README shot of a Sleep page that still can't run would be a screenshot of the bug. **Audited 2026-09-01; the file is 307 lines and stale in seven concrete ways.** (1) **"Three LEANN indexes"** (README:104-107) — LEANN is deleted; the index is a single derived, disposable sqlite-vec DB with on-device EmbeddingGemma. 5 LEANN mentions total, all wrong. (2) **"Planned extensions, but not yet part of the current implementation… Telegram ingestion, bookmark / PDF ingestion"** (README:120) — every one of those shipped, and the list is now far longer: Telegram save-with-reason, bookmarks, PDFs, RSS/Substack feeds, calendars, Apple Notes, Instagram/YouTube/Takeout/LinkedIn/TikTok/Reddit export parsers, and **live OAuth connectors for Pinterest, Reddit and X** behind one adapter registry. (3) **App views listed as "memory graph, topics, sleep dashboard, nudges, clarifications"** (README:109-114) — it is now six tabs (Graph, Clusters, Feed, Sleep, Inbox, Activity) plus a native Settings scene; nudges and clarifications collapsed into the unified Inbox two revisions ago. (4) **`swift run` is taught as the way to launch the app** (README:173-183) — `bundle.sh`'s own header explains why that is wrong: it produces a bundle-less executable whose window never becomes *key*, silently breaking graph node clicks and text-field focus. Teach `make app` / `make run-app`, which already exist. (5) The **two-terminal flow** is superseded by the launchd backend (`com.cicada.backend`, RunAtLoad+KeepAlive) — document that as the default and the manual run as the dev fallback. (6) **Thesis framing** at README:35 and :228 should match the reframed `CLAUDE.md`: started as the BSc capstone, now a personal project aimed at capturing the human experience and making it discussable by agents. (7) **Zero screenshots** — `grep '!\['` returns nothing and no `docs/images/` exists. **Screenshot method (already proven this session):** boot the launchd backend, `make app`, drive with `macos-harness` (`mac.see(app)` for capture, `mac.ax.query`/`AXPress` for navigation — raw clicks do not reach a background window, and AX scrolling is what reveals content below the fold). Capture the six tabs + Settings + the Imports catalog with its brand-logo tiles; commit to `docs/images/` and embed. **Privacy rail — this is the reason to be careful:** the live bank is the author's real memory (1,731 entities: real people, employers, family). Screenshots must come from a **demo bank**, or every frame needs review before commit; the same rule that governs `benchmarks/*.example.*` applies to anything embedded in a public README. → depends on **G74(a)** (so Sleep can be shown working) and benefits from **G88** (an installed app to screenshot); relates **G76** (the install story the README fronts). | 🔲 |
| G91 | **Share-to-Cicada — a system share target for links and documents from any app** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the importance of the companion app so that links and stuff like documents are easy to share from any app to this app") | Today capture is *pull* (paste a URL, drop a file, run an import) or the Telegram bot for mobile — there is no way to be reading something anywhere on the machine and push it in. A macOS **Share Extension** makes Cicada a first-class target in every app's share menu, which is the difference between "capture when I remember to" and "capture at the moment of interest" — the moment the *reason* is still in your head, which is exactly what G71's save-with-reason wants and what an after-the-fact import can never reconstruct. **Shape:** an `.appex` inside the app bundle declaring `NSExtensionActivationRule` for `public.url`, `public.file-url`, `public.plain-text` and PDFs; the extension POSTs straight to the already-running local backend (`POST /sources/save` for links, `/sources/upload` for files) — no app-group plumbing needed because the backend is a localhost service, which is a genuine advantage of this architecture. Add a one-field "why are you saving this?" note in the extension sheet so the `saved-because` claim (G71, already built) gets populated at the only moment the answer is cheap. **Verified constraints, and they are the whole risk:** `grep NSExtension app/` returns nothing today — no extension target exists; SwiftPM does not build `.appex` targets, so this rides on `bundle.sh`'s manual assembly (already the packaging path) or needs an Xcode project; and **Launch Services only registers a share extension for an app that is installed and code-signed** — ad-hoc signing may suffice locally but this is where the unsigned-app trade-off in **G88** stops being cosmetic. Sequence it after G88 for that reason. **iOS is a separate build, not a flag** — the Telegram bot remains the mobile answer until then. → depends on **G88** (installed, signed app), completes **G71** (reason-at-capture), relates **G62** (channel registry — the share sheet is a new channel with its own origin) and **G37**. **(Absorbs G37, merged 2026-09-01)** — the ask was first flagged as "Coming soon" in `SyncSetupView`, Track D3. | 🔲 |
| G92 | **Onboarding at scale — decide what Cicada IS as a product before optimizing the funnel** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "think how the deployment to onboard users at scale would work") | **The architecture already makes one strategic choice, and it is unusually good: compute is BYO.** G74(a) runs consolidation through the *user's own* Claude subscription, and embeddings are on-device (EmbeddingGemma). So marginal inference cost per user is **≈ zero** — no GPU bill, no per-token exposure, no rate-limit pooling. Almost no memory product can say that, and it should be treated as the core of any distribution plan rather than an implementation detail. **The tension:** every other design decision is deliberately single-user and local — filesystem as the single source of truth, one git repo per bank, no database, secrets in `~/.cicada`, an MCP server registered per machine, a launchd backend. **A hosted multi-tenant Cicada is a different product**, not a deployment of this one: it would break the storage decision, and it means holding other people's episodic memory on your infrastructure — the highest-sensitivity data class there is, with the correspondingly worst breach story. **Three honest routes, pick deliberately:** (a) **Local-first distribution** — signed + notarized `.dmg` (Apple Developer Program, notarization, an update channel), G76's paste-prompt install for the developer audience, everything stays on the user's machine. Cheapest, keeps every architectural claim intact, ceiling is "power users who can install things". (b) **Local-first + optional hosted sync** — E2E-encrypted bank sync/backup and a mobile client that reads it; the server never holds plaintext, so the storage decision survives. This is the only route where a subscription is honest, because you would be charging for *convenience and continuity*, not for inference the user already pays for. (c) **Hosted** — abandons the thesis claim; note it and move on unless the goal changes. **The real funnel is permissions, not marketing** — each of these is a drop-off point and all are already known: Gatekeeper on an unsigned app (**G88**), TCC prompts for Contacts (**G81**) and Notes, per-client MCP registration (**G76**), and a Claude/Codex login the user must already have. Instrument nothing until the route is chosen; the install is the funnel. **Prerequisites the 2026-09-01 portability sweep surfaced, neither named elsewhere:** the human observer id is the literal `rodrigo` (`agentic_write.py:325,:331-333`; no `CICADA_USER`/owner setting exists in `config.py`), so a second user's manual claims are silently NOT `user_stated`; and a bank has no manifest, schema version, or export/import-as-archive (`bank_registry.py`/`routers/banks.py` → 0 hits for `manifest|schema_version`; `POST /banks/{name}/import` imports a chat export, not a bank), while `repos:` stores tilde paths + `socket.gethostname()` device ids with no remap table (`local_refs.py:64-87`) — a bank moved to another machine keeps its pages but loses live repo resolution. The shareable artifact that needs none of this is a compiled skill bundle (**G112**). → depends on **G74(a)** (BYO compute is the premise), **G88**, **G76**; constrains **G81**, **G91**; the D1 bank-export line of `context-passport-roadmap.md` is where the manifest belongs. | 🔲 |
| G93 | **Ask/search is built — the open question is what "smart" adds, and whether any of it is chargeable** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a smart search inside cicada or ask ai inside cicada might be useful and maybe even part of a paid plan (add idea to backlog for later discussion)") | **Already shipped:** `GET /search` (cross-graph entities + episodes) and `POST /ask` (grounded NL answer with wikilink citations and gap analysis) are both mounted (`api/main.py:154-155`), and the app has a ⌘K Ask panel (G52). So this row is not "build search" — it is **what would make it smart**, and that has a precise answer from **G79**: today Ask retrieves *within* whatever the question names; the north star is **cross-stream retrieval** — surfacing a curated artifact inside an experiential context the user never explicitly linked ("you saved this three months ago and it answers the thing you're deciding now"). That is the capability worth building and the only one that is genuinely hard. Concrete gaps under it: no temporal scoping ("what was I thinking about in March"), no saved/standing queries, no proactive surfacing (Ask is strictly pull — the north star's "smartly gives" half is unbuilt), and Ask is a single-shot answer rather than a conversation over memory. **On the paid-plan idea — the honest complication, worth deciding before designing around it:** if **G74(a)**'s BYO-compute model holds, the user's *own* Claude subscription pays for the inference behind every Ask. Charging for the query while they fund the tokens is a hard sell and invites the obvious "why not just ask Claude directly". The defensible monetization is the thing the user cannot trivially self-host: **hosted E2E sync, a mobile client, continuity across devices, and a managed engine for people with no Claude/Codex subscription at all** — i.e. G92 route (b). Park the pricing question there rather than attaching it to Ask. → extends **G52** (Ask panel), **A5** (gap analysis), **D3** ("answers not pages", already decided); depends on **G79** for what "smart" means and **G92** for whether anything is charged. | 🛠️ search/ask half already shipped (`GET /search` + `POST /ask` mounted `api/main.py:154-155`; ⌘K Ask panel, G52); the cross-stream-retrieval "smart" half is open — see TODO Wave C #13. |
| G94 | **Life-data streams (wearables, Spotify, GPS/saved places, Strava) — aggregate into experience, never dump the samples** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a way where its easy to port data from other things like werables… spotify, GPS (google maps saved places), strava… CIcada should be done in such an agnostic way that it is able to ingest all this data and in turn communicate in a way thats easy for the human and agents to understand") | **The agnostic ingestion the user asks for is real and mostly already there** — `RawItem` + `origin` + the `ADAPTERS` connector seam + url_index dedup are source-agnostic by construction, and Google **Takeout parsing already exists** (`media_ingestor.parse_youtube_takeout*`), which is the same export vehicle that carries Maps saved places. Sanctioned routes per source: **Spotify** (real Web API, OAuth — saved tracks, top artists; plus a GDPR export for history), **Strava** (real API, OAuth), **Google Maps saved places** (Takeout), **Apple Health / wearables** (an on-device export XML, or HealthKit from a future iOS app — no server API), Oura/Whoop/Garmin (each has its own API). Each is one more adapter module in the existing registry — **that part is not the hard problem.** **The hard problem is representation, and getting it wrong would wreck the graph.** These streams are *quantitative time series*; Cicada's model is entities + claims + episodes, i.e. *semantic*. Dumping 8,000 heart-rate samples or every song play into a markdown knowledge graph produces a graph that is 99% noise and a Sleep queue that never drains — the same category error as dumping raw transcripts, which this project already refused. **The rule this row exists to fix:** ingest **aggregates and notable events, never samples** — "slept under 6h for nine days straight", "ran a personal best", "first visit to Lisbon", "listened to one album obsessively for a week". Raw samples stay outside the bank entirely (the `~/.cicada/` precedent for logos, screenshots and telemetry: derived-or-bulk artifacts of the outside world are not versioned memory), or are not retained at all. The closed 10-type set mostly absorbs the result without extension — **`location` already exists** for places visited, `media` for albums/tracks, and a workout or a sleep week is an *episode* with claims rather than an entity.
