Resist adding types; that instinct is what the type set is for. **Why this is worth building at all — and it is the strongest argument in the backlog for it:** the value of life data in a memory system is **correlation with experience, not the data itself**. "You were in Lisbon that week, slept five hours a night, and that is when you decided to switch projects" is exactly **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval, and no fitness app can say it because none of them hold the conversations. The summarization step — turning a numeric week into one sentence a human and an agent both read the same way — is **G77**'s output-packet idea applied to quantitative data, and it is deterministic enough to run without an engine (**G80**). **Sequence:** start with ONE source that is export-based and already half-supported (Maps saved places via Takeout → `location` entities), prove the aggregate-not-samples rule end to end, then add an OAuth adapter (Strava or Spotify) once the representation is settled. → extends **G47/G71** (ingest family + connector seam), **G79** (the north star this most directly serves), **G77** (packet-style summarization), **G80** (aggregation is engine-free), **G70** (the same "derived artifact lives outside the bank" rail). | 🔲 |
| G95 | **Meetings and human↔human conversations as an ingestion source** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "have meetings/conversations be part of ingestion, so transcriptions from conversations can also come into this as part of human experience, wether its just a transcript, a recording, etc. useful info can come from this such as the people involved, things said, ideas discussed") | Named in `CLAUDE.md` as part of what Cicada is for, and **the one intake class the pipeline was not designed for**. Every episode today is *single-observer* — the user talking to an agent, or the user saving something. A meeting is **multi-party**, and that changes three layers, not just the parser. **What already exists and can carry it:** the ingest pipeline is source-agnostic (`RawItem` + `origin` + `processed: false`, Sleep extracts regardless of source), and — verified — the claim layer **already models third parties**: `observer: str = "agent" # agent | rodrigo | external:<name>` (`api/services/claims.py:68`), with three `external:*` observers live on the current graph (`eugenio-marchiori`, `konrad`, `milan`) and a per-observer trust multiplier already in the decay engine (`claim_reconciler.py:51-53`, `user_stated` fades ~3× slower). So "who said this" has a home; nothing needs inventing. **What does not exist:** no transcript parsing of any kind (no VTT/SRT/diarized-text reader — the `transcript` hits in `entity_extractor`/`session_stats` are about agent sessions), and **no audio transcription anywhere** — the Telegram voice-note transcription described in prose is **not implemented** (`grep -i whisper api/` → nothing). **Design:** (i) **Text first** — accept a transcript (`.txt`/`.md`/`.vtt`/`.srt`, or a paste) with optional speaker labels, `origin: meeting` (or per-tool: `granola`, `zoom`, `otter`, `voice-memo`), landing as one episode. This alone delivers most of the value and needs no new infrastructure. (ii) **Recordings** — transcribe **on-device** (whisper.cpp / MLX Whisper), matching the EmbeddingGemma precedent of a keyless local default; **the audio itself never enters the bank** — it is bulk binary and belongs under `~/.cicada/recordings/` or nowhere, exactly the rail that governs logos, screenshots and telemetry. Only the transcript is memory. (iii) **Speaker → observer**: a labelled speaker maps to `external:<slug>`, so a statement by someone else is stored as *theirs*, not as the user's belief — and **their** assertion about themselves is high-trust while their assertion about the world is not. This is where **G81 (contacts)** pays off: speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to real humans, and without it every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `person`. (iv) **What to extract**: attendees → `person`; ideas discussed → `concept`; decisions and commitments → claims with `valid_from` (the bi-temporal layer is already built for exactly this); action items → the existing inbox. No new entity type — a meeting is an episode, and the closed 10-type set absorbs the rest. **The rail, and it is heavier than any other source's:** recording a conversation involves **people who never opted into your memory system**. Spain (and many jurisdictions) require all-party consent for recording; beyond legality, storing another person's words in a permanently-historied git repo and letting an agent recall them is a different ethical class from saving your own chats. Non-negotiables: consent is captured per meeting and stored with the episode; a participant can be excluded or redacted after the fact (which git history makes genuinely hard — decide the redaction story *before* building, not after); third-party PII stays minimal per the **G81** rule; and nothing about a meeting is ever surfaced outside the user's own machine. **Sequence:** transcript-drop → speaker-to-observer mapping → on-device transcription → live capture (a much bigger problem: macOS system-audio capture needs ScreenCaptureKit/TCC or a virtual device, and meeting-bot integrations are a different product surface — explicitly out of scope for now). → the human↔human half of the project goal in `CLAUDE.md`; depends on **G81** (identity anchors), extends **G20/G47** (import family) and the claim layer's `external:` observers; relates **G77** (a meeting summary is the packet idea's natural home), **G94** (same aggregate-not-raw rail), **G79** (a meeting is pure experience-stream). | 🔲 |
| G96 | *(merged into [G99](#g99) — the vector-as-entryway validation that prompted the relational-tier measurement. Full argument preserved there.)* | — | — |
| G97 | **Inbox items must show the conversation that caused them** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "clarifications for example would be better if provided some context of the conversations that mention them, so as to remind the user of what is was being talked about… same with conflicts and duplicates") | **This is a read-path data-loss bug, not missing data — the link is already on disk and is thrown away at the API boundary.** Clarification files carry `source_episode` and `source_episode_timestamp` in frontmatter (e.g. `inbox-078.md` → `ep_2026-04-15_002`, 2026-04-15), and the *resolve* path already reads `source_episode` (`inbox_service.py:590`) — but `_item_from_file` (`inbox_service.py:52-101`) maps ~18 keys and never reads it, and `InboxItem` (`schemas.py:795-819`) has no field to carry it. The app cannot show provenance because the server never sends it. **Coverage measured on the live bank: 43 of 49 items reach at least one episode** via a three-tier fallback — `item.source_episode` (8/8 clarifications) → `claim.source_episodes` (14/27 conflicts) → `entity.source_episodes` (7/27 conflicts, 14/14 merges). **Cost: 102–132 ms for all 49 items, 139 file reads, zero LLM calls, zero git calls, zero vector lookups** — so it is engine-free and Sleep-safe (**G80**). The context is genuinely the reminder asked for: `<surname-a>` and `<surname-b>` both trace to the same episode, *"<episode title A>"*; `<role at unnamed company>` to *"<episode title B>"* (real values redacted from this row). **Second cause, specific to clarifications:** the body shown in the expanded card is `source_context`, which is set to **the extractor's own one-line description**, not an episode excerpt (`entity_resolver.py:238`, `:490-493` → `clarification_manager.py:112`) — and when the extractor produced no description the body is *empty*, which is exactly the blank card in the screenshot. Fix by slicing ±N chars around the entity mention in the episode, at creation or lazily at read. **Third, the duplicated subtitle** (visible as "<name> / <name>", "OpenCode / OpenCode"): the writer sets `title = entity_name` verbatim (`clarification_manager.py:95`) and the card renders `displayName` on line 1 and `title` on line 2 for every non-conflict kind (`InboxCardView.swift:64,69`) — byte-identical strings. Replace line 2 with the episode context, and give clarifications a real title ("Who is {name}?"). **Ship-together trap:** `GET /inbox` ETags over the `inbox` component only (`inbox.py:17`), so once the response embeds entity/episode-derived context an entity edit will not change the ETag and the app will serve stale context from cache forever — widen to `("inbox","entities","episodes")` in the same commit. **Concrete bug found while probing:** `inbox-3747.md` has an unquoted colon in `uncertainty_type` (`Possible duplicate of π0.7: A Steerable…`), so `yaml.safe_load` raises and `load_inbox`'s bare `except: continue` silently drops it — **50 items on disk, 49 served**, with no log line and no way for the user to ever see or resolve it. **UNRESOLVED — the avatar complaint** ("some pfp like the one of conflicting beliefs about pi make no sense"): the first-pass explanation (LogoImage defaults to `.concept`, whose colour is green, because `InboxCardView.swift:56` passes no `type:`) had its code facts verified but its empirical claim refuted on re-check, and it does not explain the specific row named. Re-derive before fixing. What is certain: `InboxItem` carries no entity type on the wire at all, so the card *cannot* colour by type today; and a name-initial monogram is the wrong metaphor for a conflict item regardless — the kind icon already drawn at `InboxCardView.swift:58` carries more meaning. **Delivered by G115 Phase 1 (2026-09-02 ruling):** the ETag trap has a CLIENT half — `VersionVector.swift:8-9` maps `entities` to `[.graph,.contributors,.origins]` and `episodes` to `[.status,.origins,.sources,.channels]`, neither refreshing `.inbox`, so the same commit that widens `etag_for(mp, "inbox", "entities", "episodes")` must add `.inbox` to both mappings or `SnapshotCache` keeps stale context until the inbox itself moves; the shape is a `cause` object `{episode_id, timestamp, conversation_id, harness, conversation_title, excerpt, mention_offsets, tier: item|claim|entity}` resolved by the three-tier fallback measured above, offsets recomputed at read (never stored), `[ no source recorded ]` served when all tiers fail — never a hidden card; a `bank_index.parse_count` budget test guards the ~100 ms figure; clarification `title` becomes the question (`Who is {name}?`); the kind-icon change is made on the certain facts only and the pfp cause above stays unresolved. Lands ahead of its wave position for the reason G115 states. **Shipped 2026-09-03 as G115 Phase 1 (`feat/inbox-phase1`):** `inbox_context.cause_for` (three tiers, ±240-char excerpt, offsets at read), `source_episode` persisted by every writer, the clarification title is the question, both ETag halves in one commit; the budget test pins the real invariant — 50 items cost at most one frontmatter parse per episode/entity file and a second load re-parses none (`bank_index.parse_count`), with only loose wall-clock smoke bounds beside it. The pfp/avatar cause stays unresolved; the card shows the kind icon and no monogram. | ✅ |
| G98 | **Roughly half the inbox is structurally unanswerable — fix the queue before dressing it up** (found 2026-09-01 while diagnosing G97; 41-agent sweep, every claim adversarially re-checked) | **The measured headline: 22 of 49 pending items (45%) have a subject that is missing from the graph or archived, and nothing in the read path checks.** `load_inbox` filters only deferred items (`inbox_service.py:112-130`), the router filters only by kind (`inbox.py:22-26`), and `refresh_open_questions` reasons only over claims (`inbox_questions.py:127-182`). So **the decay engine archived these entities and the inbox kept asking about them** — including 7 archived conflict subjects at near-zero confidence (π0 at 0.14, π0.5 at 0.09, Octo at 0.09, Konrad Kordowski at 0.014, heat_parallel.c at 0.0). *This is very likely what the user saw when a conflict about "pi" looked nonsensical.* These items are 74 days old against `inbox_stale_after_days = 90`, so the existing staleness escalation has not reached them yet either. **Fix:** have `refresh_open_questions` close any item whose subject page is absent or `status: archived` (trigger `inbox/subject_archived`), and have `load_inbox` skip them defensively. **Removes 22 items with no UI change.** **Second: predicate multiplicity accounts for most of the conflicts.** 9 of 27 treat `uses` as single-valued when it is a set — a hybrid solver legitimately uses MPI *and* OpenMP, Hono runs on Deno, a project can be Python *and* C++; Cicada's own item offers `awake-sleep-architecture / neo4j / fastapi / react-three-js / leann-vector-index` as if a tech stack were mutually exclusive. **Fix:** mark predicates functional vs set-valued in `predicates.py` and raise conflicts only for functional ones (`works-at`, `role`, `status`); `uses`, `is-a`, `located-in` accumulate. **Third: 6 more conflicts are entity-resolution artifacts** — the two "conflicting" values are the same concept under two names (`vla` vs `vision-language-action-models`, both `type: concept`, both `active`, near-identical summaries; same for `world-models` vs `world-models-for-robotics`). One merge silently kills six questions — so **run merge before conflict in the Sleep pipeline**, and normalize the OBJECT side of a claim through the entity resolver before comparing. **Fourth, and it is the most embarrassing single item:** `inbox-1446` asks where **the user himself** works (mongodb vs supahost vs triops-solutions) but `rodrigo-sagastegui` has no entity page — the user is split across `rodrigo.md` and `rodrigo-jesus-sagastegui.md`. Resolving it would write to nothing. Cause: `entity_id` is minted as `sanitize_id(entity_name)` with **no existence check** (`clarification_manager.py:94`), even though `id_utils.resolve_entity_file` is already used a few lines later for merge targets. Refuse to write an item whose subject does not resolve. **Context:** all 49 were written in one bulk backfill on 2026-06-18 over an import of 1,197 episodes of which only 3 are `mcp` captures — 787 are bookmarks and 193 apple-notes — so the extractor was largely reasoning over third-party web content rather than the user's life. That is a one-off condition, but it is why the queue reads the way it does. **Two upstream gates remove ~26 of 49 without touching a pixel**, which is why this row outranks G97: 49 well-rendered unanswerable questions is still an inbox nobody opens. → fixes the input side of **G60** (question objects), unblocks **G97**, all deterministic (**G80**); relates **G65(a)** (slug collisions) and **G21** (dedup sweep). **2026-09-03 evidence:** a project's `uses` predicate rendered a 7-option conflict card (architecture concept vs database vs three frameworks) — multi-valued predicates must never open a conflict; see G115 for the card-side rule. **Card-side rule shipped 2026-09-03 (G115 Phase 1):** `predicates.cardinality` (bank map ∪ committed seed, `multi` winning — `install_predicate_map` never refreshes a populated map, so a bank seeded before commit `e9a7c6b` still calls `uses` single-valued; anything in neither list is `unknown` and still asked), `write_claim_nudges` skips `multi` (counted), `load_inbox` serves an existing multi-valued conflict as `informational` (list + `Got it`, no claim touched). The subject-gone gate and the entity-resolution half are unchanged and still open here. | 🔲 |
| G99 | **A relational tier — DECLINED at this scale; the derived tier just has to become actually derived** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, following G96: "in what way can the addition of sqlite as relational db benefit cicada… and if we should add it to the task queue"; answered by a 4-lens measured sweep + an adversarial rebuttal, every headline number re-run against the live bank) | **Verdict: do NOT build SQL over memory. Cicada does not need a relational tier at today's scale — it needs the SQLite file it already ships to stop being versioned, stale, and undetectably wrong.** **Three of the four flagship "SQL wins" collapsed under measurement:** (a) `GET /conversations/{id}` at 1,625 ms is not a lookup problem — `bank_index.IndexedFile.body()` re-reads and re-parses every page while the same object already caches frontmatter by `(mtime_ns, size)`; raw reads of all 1,731 entities cost 24 ms, `body()` 626 ms, `parse_claims()` 876 ms. It is PyYAML, not the filesystem. Memoizing parsed claims under the key `bank_index` already uses gives **1,528 ms → ~6 ms (~250×) in ~10 lines**, and cannot be stale by construction. (b) The "perspectival retrieval silently returns nothing" bug lives in a helper with **zero production callers that pass filters**. (c) `graph_edges.yaml` (696 KB, 6,741 edges) parses in 545 ms with `yaml.safe_load`, **88.7 ms with `CSafeLoader`, 1.8 ms as JSON** — a 6,741-row list needs no query engine; only the *atomicity* half survives, and the fix is already written in-repo (`logo_service.py:483-498`, tmp + `os.replace`). **Only two things genuinely survive.** (i) **Filtered KNN is impossible, not slow** — `vector_index.py:182` stores metadata as a JSON blob, so the `timestamp` the episode index already computes and the `type`/`status`/`confidence` the entity index already computes are unqueryable; no router exposes a date parameter and `cicada_recall` takes `{query}` alone, so *"what was I reading in March"* cannot be asked at any price. sqlite-vec (already imported) supports `vec0` metadata columns — ~30 lines, inside a file whose own docstring calls itself disposable. (ii) **Life-data aggregation is inexpressible** — 20,000 one-file-per-sample markdown records = 2.7 MB of content occupying **78.1 MB on disk** (block amplification) and 2.67 s to scan; the same rows in SQLite with an index = 1.6 MB and **1.58 ms**. But G94's own rule (aggregates, never samples) means the *graph* never holds a series — what has nowhere to live is the raw store you aggregate *from*. **URGENT sub-item, and it is a bug not architecture — G99a, do it BEFORE G74 restores Sleep:** `vector_index.db` (**35 MB) is tracked in the live bank's git** — verified: `git ls-files` lists it, the bank has **no `.gitignore`** (`scaffold_bank` writes none), and `commit_changes` runs `git add -A` (`git_service.py:841`). The bank's `.git` is **69 MB against 16 MB of markdown**; `duplicate_bank` copies it verbatim so 30 MB becomes commit #1 of every new bank; and because `_rebuild_table` drops and rebuilds, **a nightly Sleep would commit a fresh ~30 MB blob every night (~11 GB/yr) the moment the engine lands.** It is also **silently stale**: db mtime 2026-07-14 while **636 entity pages are newer**, `index_meta` stores only `{model, dim}`, and `/status`'s `_leann_present` is a bare existence check that reports it healthy. G99a = bank `.gitignore` for `vector_index.db`/`-wal`/`-shm`, `git rm --cached` on existing banks, exclude derived artifacts from `duplicate_bank`, add a `source_version` (bank HEAD + content hash) checked on open and surfaced by `/status`; plus `journal_mode=WAL` + `synchronous=NORMAL` (`_connect` sets **no PRAGMA at all**) and stop the three `except sqlite3.OperationalError: return []` handlers from turning a lock into "the agent has no memory" with no log line. **Ordered tail:** G99b memoize parsed claims (~10 lines; test: warm `/conversations/{id}` p50 < 20 ms); G99c `CSafeLoader` + port the atomic-write pattern to the five hand-rolled stores (`save_url_index`, `notes_sync`, `calendar_registry`, `sync_state`, `inbox_generator`); **G99d shipped (feat/saved-at, 2026-09-01)** — `RawItem.added` is now plumbed end to end: `api/services/saved_at.py` normalizes each of the five parsers' source-specific formats (Netscape epoch seconds, Chrome's WebKit microseconds, Takeout's ISO-8601, TikTok's `Date`, LinkedIn's drifting column) to an ISO date, persisted as a distinct top-level `saved_at:` on episode/entity frontmatter and as `content_saved_at` in `url_index.json`/`MediaSourceItem`/Swift's `MediaFeedItem`, with `GET /sources` and the Feed's Recent sort preferring it over the ingest timestamp (parsed to a real instant, not a raw-string compare, so same-day ties are deterministic). **0% of the 789 existing items had a recoverable save date** — checked `url_index.json`, episode bodies, `~/Library/Application Support/Cicada/upload_history.json`, and common export-file locations; the original `add_date`/`date_added` values were discarded at ingest time by the pre-fix code and never persisted anywhere to recover from. Historical items therefore carry no `saved_at` by design (never a guessed one) rather than a wrong one, and the fix is forward-looking only — it takes effect on the next fresh import, not retroactively on the live bank; G99e promote `vec0` metadata to real columns and expose `since`/`until`/`type` on `/search`, `/ask` and `cicada_recall` (~1 day, the one genuine SQLite win available now). **Explicitly NOT queued:** relational tables in `vector_index.db`, or migrating `url_index.json`/`sync_state.json`/`feeds.yaml`/`calendars.yaml`/`banks.yaml`/`graph_edges.yaml`/the in-page claims blocks — 3-to-789-row human-scale files whose last commits read "+9 insertions", where SQLite would trade `git diff`, `grep` and `git blame` (the substrate `/entities/{id}/history` and `/contributors` are *built on*) for query power nobody needs. **Concurrency strengthens the decline:** three processes touch a bank but there is exactly **one writer** today (Sleep); a full rebuild is a 0.71 s transaction and 40 concurrent read probes saw zero errors. The proposal would *create* the footgun it claims to solve — capture-time tables written from both MCP and API turn a single-writer batch file into three-process multi-writer under `journal_mode=delete`, and a Sleep-rebuilt claim table would be **75 days stale right now**, replacing a correct-by-construction 6 ms read with a 0.2 ms wrong one. **The invariant, applicable without re-reading any of this: markdown+git is the only source of truth; a SQLite table may exist only if deleting every `.db` costs CPU time and never a fact, and no derived artifact is ever tracked in a bank's git or copied by a bank operation.** **Triggers to revisit — named metrics, not vibes:** T1 after G99b, warm p50 still > 250 ms ⇒ revisit a derived claims table; T2 claims > ~25,000 (10× today) or entities > 10,000; **T3 (the only real forcing function) one merged G94 adapter retaining raw samples above ~50,000 rows/yr ⇒ build `~/.cicada/streams/<bank>.db` — outside every bank and outside git, on the logos/telemetry rail — whose only output into memory is an aggregated episode plus claims, which costs the thesis claim nothing**; T4 the day a second process writes the index, WAL stops being optional and the write path needs `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. |  **(Absorbs G96, merged 2026-09-01)** — Cicada already implements the "vector as entryway, not full retrieval" pattern Ed Honour's argument called for: `cicada_recall` runs a sqlite-vec search and returns entry points (`suggested_entities`, a `relevant_hub` with `hub_members_preview`, `next_tool: cicada_recall_detail`, `mcp/server.py:823-855`), after which the agent traverses wikilinks and hub pages (`_index.md` → `hubs/` → `entities/`); this row's four-lens measurement is what settled the one place Cicada deliberately disagrees with him (no relational store). **Decided 2026-09-01** — **G99a shipped** (`api/services/bank_registry.py` — bank `.gitignore` for `vector_index.db`/`-wal`/`-shm`, `git rm --cached` on existing banks, derived artifacts excluded from `duplicate_bank`); **G99d shipped**; G99b, G99c, G99e S/XS; streams DB gated on T3; general relational tier **declined** |
| G100 | **Span citation — record WHICH SENTENCE convinced the contributor, and render it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "maybe this can be the contributor who decides the part that influenced it to make the addition/substraction/modification… have a view that shows the raw conversation text and only the relevant portion highlighted… reference the conversation as notebooklm references the files") | **The gap, stated precisely: Cicada's provenance stops at DOCUMENT granularity.** `source_episodes` says *which* episode, `git blame` + the commit trailers say *which model, which session, which trigger* — but nothing records **which sentence**. So "why does Cicada believe this?" is answerable only as "somewhere in this wall of text". Verified: `grep -ci 'span citation|char offset'` over this backlog → **0**; `cicada_write_claim` takes `sources: [str]` (a *where to look it up* ref, G61) and has **no quote or span parameter**. **Design.** (i) **The contributor cites at write time** — the agent knows what convinced it; asking later is reconstruction. Add an optional `quote` (and/or `[start,end]` offsets into the named episode) to `cicada_write_claim` and the `agentic_write` path. (ii) **Store both offset and quote**: offsets are compact and render in O(1); the quote is self-verifying, so if the offset no longer matches, fall back to searching for the quote — a repair path, not a failure. (iii) **Derived spans are a SEPARATE CLASS from asserted ones.** For the ~2,300 claims that predate this, candidate spans can be *found* by fuzzy/regex matching an entity's name and aliases against its `source_episodes` — useful, and it covers the user's "not an exact mention but like a quote" case — but it must render visibly differently. One is evidence the writer stood behind; the other is a guess the reader must check. Never merge the two. (iv) **The viewer is the existing DiffView pattern pointed at conversation text**: raw episode body, the cited span highlighted, and **prev/next across every conversation that contributed to this belief** — the NotebookLM behaviour the user described. G48 already makes "which conversations" reconstructable (`session_id` on episodes + `entity_sources.py`), so this layer only adds *where inside each one*. **The dependency to verify before building: episodes must be immutable.** Capture is append-only and no rewrite path was found in a first pass, but `mark_processed` flips frontmatter on the same file — confirm it rewrites frontmatter only and never reflows the body, or offsets silently rot. If immutability cannot be guaranteed, store the quote as primary and treat offsets as a cache. **Why this is worth more than it looks:** it is the difference between a memory system that *claims* provenance and one that can be audited a sentence at a time — and it is the natural home for the "which part of the conversation influenced this" question that no other personal-memory system answers. → extends **G48** (session identity), **G61** (`sources:` — distinct: G61 is *where to re-check a fact*, this is *what originally said it*), **G67** (DiffView, the renderer to reuse); prerequisite-ish for **G101**; strengthens the thesis's provenance claim. **Absorbed into G118 (2026-09-03):** design point (i) — the contributor cites at write time — shipped as G118 slice 1 (`evidence: [{episode, quote}]` on `cicada_write_claim`, `evidence_quote` in Stage 1); (ii) is settled the other way (offsets + hash only, never the quote — the bank holds the text, `stale` replaces the repair path); the immutability dependency is verified for the body (`markdown_parser.parse` strips it identically on every rewrite; `mark_processed` never reflows it). (iii) derived spans and (iv) the viewer stay open under G118 slices 2+. | ⤴ absorbed into G118 — (i)/(ii) shipped in slice 1 (PR #44); (iii)/(iv) open |
| G101 | **Raw conversations as an evidence layer — decide what to keep, and prove Sleep is still needed** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "is sleep really needed anymore? is it because we process and save all the raw and real conversations? and could we even do this as an extra layer of context backup or provenance… is there a practical way to store all conversations smartly?") | **The answer to the question, recorded because it is a founding-decision-level judgment: raw storage does NOT replace Sleep, it makes Sleep auditable.** Sleep is *not* needed for retrieval — semantic search over raw text is RAG and it works. It is needed for four things raw text structurally cannot do: (1) **identity resolution** — "Mongo" / "MongoDB" / "the database we switched to" is a judgment, not a lookup; (2) **contradiction and decay** — *text cannot decay*; only a belief can go stale, be superseded, or be contradicted, so "time as a signal" requires a belief object to attach time to, and a transcript is a permanent fact about what was said, never about what is still true; (3) **a stable address** for confidence, sources and history to accumulate across dozens of conversations; (4) **compression** — measured in the G76 audit: 2.4 MB of curated memory versus 238 MB of transcripts for a single project (~100-300×, mostly tool-output noise). **What to build.** Store the *human-readable conversation*, never the raw harness transcript — that ratio is the whole argument, and the G48 spec invariant already forbids transcript content in a bank. Whole conversations are already reconstructable by grouping episodes on `session_id`, so the open questions are narrower than they look: **what to retain** (turns yes; tool calls, tool output and file dumps no), **where it lives** (episodes are already in the bank; anything bulkier belongs outside it on the logos/telemetry rail), **dedup** (content-hash, same as `url_index`), and **whether an unconsolidated conversation is browsable** before Sleep touches it. **The payoff is G100:** once the raw side is stored deliberately rather than incidentally, a citation can point into it, and every belief becomes traceable to the sentence that produced it — including for a future agent auditing a contributor's work, which is the user's stated motivation. → answers the "is Sleep needed" question for the thesis; depends on **G48**, feeds **G100**, bounded by **G76**'s privacy measurement and **G94/G95**'s aggregate-not-raw rail. | 🔲 |
| G102 | **Site recon — read a saved page to derive RELATIONS, not to summarize it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, restating an earlier ask: "from the bookmarks and websites is important to do some recon to see what the site is about to be able to derive relationships with other nodes") | **Partly built, and the gap is specific.** Saved links already get OpenGraph enrichment at ingest (`link_enrichment.py`, `media_ingestor.enrich`) — that is why Feed rows carry titles, sites and favicons. What does **not** exist is reading the page to derive *graph relations*: today a bookmark lands as an island with a title, and nothing connects it to the concepts, tools, people or projects it is actually about. **The framing that makes this worth building: the output is ENTITIES, not a summary.** A summary is a nicer card; extracted entities routed through Stage-2 resolution are new edges — which is what turns 789 saved links from a list into part of the graph, and is a direct prerequisite for **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval ("you saved this three months ago and it answers what you are deciding now" is impossible while saves are unlinked). **Constraints, both learned the hard way here:** (i) it is a network fetch per URL, so it belongs on the **Sleep tail** behind the same opt-out gate as connectors — never at capture time, never blocking a save; (ii) the **ToS rail** applies exactly as it did for LinkedIn and X — some sites may be fetched and parsed, some may not, and the rule is already written down: no scraping behind auth, no circumventing a block. Also relevant: **G86**'s junk-title finding (148 Google consent interstitials collapsed onto one entity) means recon must skip consent/interstitial pages or it will happily extract entities from a cookie banner. **Cheap first slice:** for already-enriched items, run extraction over the OG title+description that is *already stored* — zero new fetches, immediate edges, and it proves the resolution path before any crawling is added. → extends **G47/G71** (the saves that need relating), **G22** (media previews), **Stage 1/2** (extraction + resolution as the consumer); prerequisite for **G70** (design identity) and a direct enabler of **G79**; gated by **G86** (junk titles) and the connector network gate. **Cheap slice shipped (2026-09-02, `feat/link-summaries`).** Corrected finding first: Stage 5.57 already globbed the whole bank — the zero-`describes` result came from the pass running only after Stage 5 (never on an idle night), taking the 20 most-recent pages, and never retrying an `enrichment_attempted` page. Shipped: `link_enrichment.backfill` on the Sleep tail (`link_enrich_backfill_per_cycle`/night, oldest-first, dated fetch backoff, junk retired via `classify_page`) + `POST /maintenance/enrich-links?limit=N`; recon in `link_recon.py` — the existing Stage-1 prompt over stored title+description, 8 links per call, surface-form attribution, `entity_resolver.match_existing` (the Stage-2 judgment without `resolve()`'s creates/clarifications), `about` claims on the media page projected by Stage 5.7, unmatched mentions as pending candidates; `GET /sources` carries `description`/`about`. **Not shipped (this row stays open):** page fetching beyond OG+visible-text (JS-rendered pages, YouTube transcripts, PDFs), any `about` edge from the target's side, in-app recon controls, and relating a link to a *pending* candidate once it promotes (today the candidate carries the link's episode as its source, so promotion backfills context but not the edge — a follow-up). Final review (2026-09-03) closed four of the disclosed gaps: the endpoint now holds a process-local lock (409 on overlap, alongside the R11 Sleep guard); `GET /sources` strips the ```claims fence before excerpting (the row was carrying serialized claim YAML on every backfilled page); recon leaves an existing pending candidate untouched rather than replacing a conversation's provenance with a blurb's; and `llm_calls` counts answered calls only, so an engine abort no longer stamps a model on a zero-LLM commit. Still disclosed, not fixed: a page whose extractor returned a malformed non-engine response is stamped `recon_attempted` after one try — only engine failures (R9) leave pages unmarked. | 🛠️ cheap slice shipped |
| G103 | **Surface the observer model in the UI — whose belief is this, and who was in the room** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a small detail in the UI that makes reference to the model of entity observer relations (like who was in the conversation, who's belief, etc). this goes in hand with the import of conversations") | The data model already supports this and the UI barely admits it. Every claim carries `observer` — `agent | rodrigo | external:<name>` (`claims.py:68`) — the graph aggregates per-node observer sets (`graph_builder.py:115-122`), and the decay engine already weights trust per observer. But the only surface is the **All / Cicada / You / External** segmented control, which **G84(d)** found filters on claim observer, dims rather than filters, and returns true unconditionally for the 56% of nodes carrying no claims — so three of its four segments look identical and it communicates nothing. **What to build:** (i) on an **entity card**, show per-claim attribution inline — "you said this", "Cicada inferred this from a conversation", "heard from <person>" — so a page stops reading as one undifferentiated set of facts; (ii) on a **conversation** (Activity ▸ Conversations, and the import flow), show **who was present** and let a belief be traced to the participant who asserted it; (iii) relabel the observer control to what it means (G84(d) already proposes `Everything · Inferred by Cicada · Stated by you · Heard from others`) and gate segments on non-empty membership. **Why it pairs with conversation import, as the user says:** an imported agent chat has exactly one human observer, so today's model is adequate and invisible; the moment **G95** lands and a *meeting* is imported, a single episode carries several humans, and "whose belief is this" stops being cosmetic and becomes the difference between recording what *you* think and what someone told you. Building the surface now — while the answer is simple — means the multi-party case has somewhere to render rather than forcing a UI scramble later. **Dependency:** identity is only meaningful once speakers resolve to real people (**G81** contacts), otherwise every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `external:` observer. → extends **G84(d)** (the observer control), the claim layer's existing `observer`/`source_trust`; pairs with **G95** (meetings), depends on **G81** (contacts) for names that mean anything; supports **G100** (a citation is stronger when it names who said it). **(iv) First surface (G115 Phase 2):** each inbox option carries `observer` / `authored_by` from its claim, rendered through one `observer_label(observer, owner)` — `you said` / `Cicada inferred (<model>)` / `heard from <name>` — and the owner literal comes from a new `observer_owner` setting (default `"user"`) replacing the hardcoded string at `inbox_service.py:813`; a conflict card then shows the two disagreeing sentences side by side with who said each. | 🔲 |
| G104 | **A resumed conversation is consolidated twice, as if it were two** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "if i was working on a claude code conversation yesterday > slept > then continued in that conversation, is important that the system knows it is continuing") | **Verified: the mechanics exist, nothing uses them.** `session_id` is stamped at capture (G48) and `sleep_cycle._collect_session_ids` gathers them into `Cicada-Session:` trailers — but episode *selection* is purely `processed: false` (`sleep_cycle.py`), so Sleep never asks whether a conversation is still open. The failure sequence: work Monday → Sleep consolidates Monday's episodes and marks them processed → continue the **same** session Tuesday → Sleep consolidates the second half **with no awareness it already drew conclusions from the first**. Two concrete harms: (i) the **promotion threshold** is "referenced in 2+ separate conversations", and one split conversation can satisfy it, promoting an entity that was mentioned twice in a single sitting; (ii) a belief formed from partial context is never revisited when the rest of the conversation arrives — the extraction that saw half the story stands as if it saw all of it. **The tension is that `CLAUDE.md` already anticipated this** ("Conversations don't have clean endings. Batch sees patterns across full day") — but that reasoning assumed a conversation is *over* by nightfall, and G48's resumable sessions break the assumption. **REFINED 2026-09-01 (Rodrigo: "should see that either the first chunk produced something or it didnt, if it did re-check and add or remove or keep the same… or just reconsolidate everything?"). The incremental version is probably right, because most of the reconciliation ALREADY EXISTS:** `claim_reconciler` stamps `valid_to`/`superseded_by` when a new claim replaces an old one (`claim_reconciler.py:138-139`), and Wave-1's predicate work decides whether a second value contradicts or accumulates. So claims extracted from the second half already flow through add/supersede/reinforce **without re-reading the first half**. That narrows the gap to three things: (1) the **promotion threshold miscounts** — "2+ separate conversations" is satisfied by one split conversation; count distinct `session_id`s, not episodes (cheap); (2) nothing marks a belief as **formed from partial context**, so a claim from an open conversation is indistinguishable from one from a finished conversation — a provisional flag would let the later extraction supersede rather than merely contradict; (3) **summaries do not reconcile, only claims do** — the entity body is synthesized prose (`conflict_resolver._synthesize_entity_update:642`), so a summary written from half a conversation is NOT repaired by adding claims and must be regenerated. **Working split: claims incremental, summaries regenerated**, with full reconsolidation reserved for the case where the second half contradicts the first — which claim supersession already detects, so it can trigger itself. Cost argues the same way: with the CLI engine Stage 2 is sequential and dominates, so "re-extract everything nightly" is the wrong default. **Candidate solutions, cheapest first:** (a) **defer** — skip episodes whose session is still resumable and idle less than N hours, letting the conversation settle before consolidating (cheap, no new machinery, delays value by up to a day); (b) **treat a session as the promotion unit** — count distinct `session_id`s rather than episodes when evaluating the 2+-conversations threshold (very cheap, fixes harm (i) only); (c) **reconsolidation — the likeliest right answer**: when a conversation grows after having been consolidated, re-run extraction over the *whole* session and supersede the earlier claims rather than appending to them. The claim layer already supports this exactly — `valid_to` + `superseded_by` is precisely "this belief was replaced by a better-informed one" — so reconsolidation is less new machinery than it sounds, and it generalises beyond resumed sessions to *any* case where better evidence arrives later. Cost: extraction is re-run over already-processed episodes, so it needs a bound (re-consolidate a session at most once more, or only while it stays resumable). → depends on **G48** (session identity, already shipped); touches the promotion model and Stage 1/2; relates **G101** (the raw layer that would make a re-run cheap) and **G74** (the engine that must exist for any re-extraction to be possible). | 🔲 |
| G105 | **Deterministic conversation extraction — stop making the model decide what an episode is** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "instead of giving the whole power to the model to detect episodes, can't sleep see what apps we have connected, and extract the conversations from them? with a deterministic export script") | **The observation is correct and it explains the capture gap.** Capture today is entirely **agent-judgment**: `cicada_save_episode` is an MCP tool an agent must *choose* to call, so what reaches memory is whatever the model decided was worth keeping — and when no agent chooses, nothing is captured at all. Measured twice this session: the MCP server logged **zero tool invocations across 12 days**, and one very long working session contributed **4 episodes**. That is not a broken server; nothing prompts an agent to use it. **The proposal — Sleep enumerates connected apps and extracts conversations deterministically — is a direct application of G80's deterministic rung** and would make capture a property of the system rather than of a model's diligence. **What makes it feasible:** the sources are already enumerable — `connectors.ADAPTERS` plus the channel registry know what is connected, and the harnesses keep their own on-disk conversation stores (`~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`, `~/.codex/sessions`, and for Claude Desktop an export). A per-source extractor is a parser, not a judgment: read the store, group turns into conversations, emit episodes. **The rail that must not be crossed, and it is already written down:** the G48 spec forbids transcript *content* in a bank, and G76 measured why — 2.4 MB of curated memory versus 238 MB of transcripts for one project, mostly tool output and secrets. So the extractor keeps **human-readable turns** and discards tool calls, tool output and file dumps; the judgment moves from "is this worth remembering" (a model decision, currently unmade) to "is this conversation content" (a parser decision, deterministic). **RESCOPED 2026-09-01 by Rodrigo's objection ("a lot of the things generated in claude code are just commands and code, so what is relevant to process?") — and it is correct: the naive version fails on coding harnesses.** For a coding session the code and commands are **already memorialized in the repo and its git history**; Cicada storing a bash command duplicates git badly. What git does NOT hold is the reasoning — why something was declined, what was rejected, what the user prefers — and that is the only part worth extracting. So: (i) the deterministic step is a **strong pre-filter, not a selector** — dropping every `tool_use`/`tool_result` block is purely structural and removes the overwhelming bulk (most of the 238 MB vs 2.4 MB gap), but the remaining prose is still largely narration, so a deterministic pass alone would capture noise; (ii) **scope per-harness, not uniformly** — a Claude Desktop / ChatGPT conversation is *discourse* and bulk turn-extraction is reasonable there, while a Claude Code session is *mechanics* and bulk extraction is wrong; (iii) for coding harnesses the high-precision signal is that **a commit is a decision boundary** — the session already produced commits with messages, and G48 already links sessions to commits via trailers, so extracting around what a session PRODUCED beats extracting everything it said; (iv) and the **moment of writing beats reconstruction** (the same argument as G100) — for Claude Code the higher-yield fix is likely G76's TRIGGER-block rewrite, firing capture when the user states a decision or preference, rather than archaeology over a transcript. **Pairs with the G76 hook design** rather than replacing it: hooks say *a session ended and here is its pointer*; this says *here is what was in it*, without an agent in the loop. **Small, high-value companion — surface provenance in the queue:** the Sleep page's episode queue should show each episode with its **source logo** (Claude Code, Codex, Claude Desktop, Telegram, Pinterest…) — the brand assets already exist from G71's tile work and `origin` is already on every episode, so it is a rendering change that makes "where did this come from" answerable at a glance. → extends **G80** (deterministic rung), **G76** (always-on capture — this is its non-hook half), **G62/G71** (channel registry + logo assets); bounded by **G48**'s no-transcript-content invariant; feeds **G101**. **RULING (Rodrigo, 2026-09-03): extract block-level, not turn-level.** Claude Code's transcript is typed blocks, so the extractor keeps exactly (a) the person's turns — `user` messages whose blocks are `text` (a `user` message carrying a `tool_result` is tool output wearing the user role and is dropped) — and (b) the agent's **final reply per turn**: the last assistant `text` block(s) before the next human message; interstitial narration between tool calls, every `tool_use`/`tool_result`/thinking block and every file dump are skipped by construction. On what survives: fenced code stripped, per-turn cap (~2,000 chars) and per-session cap, a secret scrubber before any write. Runs from the session-end hook with the transcript path the harness passes (G76's hook half); Codex's session store gets the same block filter; Claude Desktop/ChatGPT keep their export importers. Each kept turn is an episode turn, so G118 spans cite it. **G48's rail is restated, not removed:** what never enters a bank is tool output, code and secrets — the person's own words and the agent's replies to them are the experience Cicada exists to capture. Owner's fallback if the assistant half proves noisy in practice: keep only the person's turns (one flag). **Shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #46, `feat/deterministic-capture`):** `api/services/transcript_extract.py` (R4 boundary rule: any user-role message without a `tool_result` is a boundary, a `tool_use` resets the pending reply; R5 harness-tag filter per block; R6 order fences → scrub → per-turn cap → head-stable session cap); `POST /capture/transcript` + `transcript_capture.py` (R2 path validation under the harness root, `<session_id>.jsonl`, 256 MiB cap; R3 one episode per `(capture_kind: transcript, session_id)`, updated in place with `processed: false` and `processed_by` popped; R10 counts-only `capture` ledger row); the `Stop` hook (R1 — `SessionEnd` never fires on a closed window and shares a 1.5 s budget; the last Stop is the end) with an idempotent settings-merge registry driven by `install.sh` / `--uninstall` / `make doctor`; R8 `CICADA_CAPTURE=off` on every Cicada CLI spawn so Sleep's own prompts are never captured; the Sleep queue's source marks. **Open:** Claude Desktop / ChatGPT stay export-based; Cursor and other harnesses have no hook; Codex's Stop payload is registered but unverified hands-on (the hook logs `skipped: no transcript_path` if it lacks the field); no backfill over past transcripts by design (the only read is the session that just ended). Owner's fallback if the assistant half is noisy: `CICADA_CAPTURE_ASSISTANT_REPLIES=false`. | ✅ |
| G106 | **Two-way navigation between conversations and the entities they produced** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a page in the UI for the user to only see and search raw conversations… and the entities that were benefitted from the information there, so that you can track both ways") | The pieces exist and are not connected into a loop. Today: `source_episodes` links an entity to its episodes, `entity_sources.py` resolves episodes back to whole conversations, G48 gives conversations identity and resumability, and `/conversations/recent` lists them — but there is **no surface that goes both ways**, and no way to search conversations by their *content*. **What to build, as one loop.** (i) A **Conversations page**: browse and full-text search raw conversations by what was said, not just by title or date — the vector index already holds episode embeddings, so semantic search over conversations is largely a query away, with a literal/regex mode alongside it for exact recall. (ii) From a conversation, **list the entities it produced or updated** — derivable today by inverting `source_episodes` — each clickable through to its entity page. (iii) From an **entity**, show the conversation snippets that justify it, each clickable through to that conversation *at the right place*. Step (iii) is where **G100**'s span citation pays off: without it the link lands on a wall of text; with it, the exact sentence is highlighted, and prev/next walks every contributing conversation — the NotebookLM behaviour the user asked for. **Why this is more than a browser:** it makes the memory system *falsifiable by the user*. Today a belief is a summary you either trust or do not; with the loop closed, any belief can be traced to the sentence that produced it, and any conversation shows what it taught Cicada — which is the honest version of "transparency over magic" (UX principle 2), and the strongest possible answer to "why does it think that". **Sequencing:** the inverse index (ii) is cheap and works today; content search is next; (iii) is gated on G100. Ship them in that order rather than waiting for all three. → depends on **G100** (span citation) for the deep link, **G101** (what raw content is retained) for what there is to search; extends **G48** (conversation identity + resume), **G52** (Ask, which should cite into this same viewer); serves **G79** (cross-stream retrieval needs the user to be able to *see* the cross-stream link). **The inbox is the first caller (G115):** its `c` / "Open conversation" needs (i)'s page to land on a conversation with the cause episode scrolled into view, and its `e` / "Open entity" already works through `graphVM.selectEntity(id:)`; ship the inverse index (ii) and the conversation-with-anchor route before G115 Phase 2, or "Open conversation" opens a list instead of a place. | 🔲 |
| G107 | **Tamagotchi-style status mascot — and the honest asset problem** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "i agree… have a tamagotchi style mascot for info in status. And in regards to mascot, check it because i remember it being a bit shit") | **The framing, recorded as the user's reasoning for future sessions:** a mascot is not decoration — it is a **status surface**. A tamagotchi communicates state (fed / neglected / asleep / happy) at a glance and gives the user a *relationship* with the system's condition, which is a much better fit for "sleep debt" than a number in a table. Cicada's whole metaphor is biological, so a creature that visibly needs sleep is the most honest possible rendering of an unconsolidated queue. **The asset problem is real and structural — the user's memory is correct.** Inspected 2026-09-01: `MenuBar/BookwormSprites.swift` is a **~16×16 monochrome pixel grid** (`headOpen` is 6 rows × 16 chars — literally two eyes) rendered by `BookwormRenderer` into a **template `NSImage`**, i.e. an asset designed for the macOS **menu bar**: tiny, single-colour, tinted by the system. It is then reused at large sizes — `FeedView.swift:207` renders it at `pointSize: 72`, `ConnectView.swift:244` and `UploadOverlay.swift:96` likewise — where a 16-px monochrome glyph inevitably reads as a blown-up low-res icon with no personality. **Two consequences that decide the work:** (i) a full-page mascot needs a **different asset class** — higher-resolution multi-frame art (colour, not template), a composed vector character, or an illustrated set; scaling the menu-bar sprite will never look good, and no amount of animation fixes the source resolution; (ii) **template mode cannot express mood** — the whole point is that a sleep-deprived face should *look* different, and a template image is tinted uniformly by the system, so colour and expression are unavailable by construction. Keep the existing sprite for the menu bar (it is correct there) and treat the page mascot as a new asset. **Interim decision (Rodrigo, 2026-09-01): until real art exists, the Sleep page shows the state as TEXT IN BRACKETS** — `[ awake ]`, `[ sleeping · stage 2 of 5 ]`, `[ 47 episodes behind ]` — monospaced, theme colours, no ASCII art and no emoji. It sidesteps the scaling problem entirely and looks deliberate rather than unfinished; the state machine is unchanged, only the presentation. The existing `BookwormView` stays exactly as-is at its current call sites (menu bar, Feed, Connect, UploadOverlay). **States already modelled** (`BookwormState.swift`) and worth keeping: `awake`, `sleeping(stage:)`, `digesting`, `happy`, `curious(count:)`, `hungry` — `hungry` maps directly onto sleep-deprived, so the state machine is done and only the art is missing. **Deferred from the Sleep-screen build (2026-09-01):** per-cycle **time estimates**, because a duration is only meaningful once the consolidating model is settled — per-episode wall clock differs enormously between the CLI rung (sequential subprocess spawns), a local Ollama model and a hosted API, so estimating now would bake in a number to unlearn. Revisit once **G74**'s engine story stabilises and `sleep_run` telemetry has real history (currently **zero** events recorded). → extends **G28** (the shipped menu-bar bookworm), the Sleep-screen work; relates **G77** (a mascot is the visual twin of the output-packet idea — state a human reads instantly) and **G54** (an onboarding companion has the same asset need). **Interim ruling superseded 2026-09-02 (Rodrigo: "work on doing a pixel bookworm mascot with animations so in every state it's always moving … I want just one Tamagotchi-like avatar of the bookworm that moves and shows the status").** **Shipped 2026-09-02 (`feat/mascot`, PR #36):** one code-defined 24×24 palette sprite set (`MenuBar/BookwormSprites.swift`, nine colours — outline, body, belly, eye, blush, accent, zZ, ?/badge, error red — composed from shared head/glasses/mouth/body fragments so the silhouette is identical across moods); every state ≥ 2 frames that differ (awake bob+blink, sleeping zZ drift + chest rise, digesting chews a book, curious tilts with a pulsing ? and the count as a pixel numeral in a pill, hungry droops with a sweat drop, happy bounces with sparkles, and a new **`error`** state — red pupils + glitch frame — for `sleep.error != nil`, outranking everything but a running cycle); intervals 0.3–0.7 s. `BookwormRenderer` draws COLOUR (not template) nearest-neighbour with one cache keyed state|frame|count|stage|size. The **menu bar is one animated item** — the `button.title` text badge that duplicated the count is gone, the count lives in the sprite, the timer runs for every state, Reduce Motion holds frame 0. `BookwormView` renders the same frames on a `TimelineView` at whole-cell sizes (48/96/120) on Feed, Connect, Inbox, the import overlay and the Sleep page, where the bracket status line stays as the **caption** under a 120 pt worm. Not built, on purpose: sound, drag, feeding mechanics, any toggle beyond Reduce Motion. Per-cycle time estimates stay deferred on G74's trigger. **`reading` shipped 2026-09-05 as part of G125** (`feat/study-desk`) — a Sleep-page-only state (`deriveSleepPageMood`, not the menu bar's `deriveBookwormState`) for intake being consumed; the menu bar's own states and precedence are unchanged. | ✅ |
| G108 | **Landing page and navigation — is the graph really the front door?** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "i believe that might have to be the main page, not graph. graph should follow. Or maybe that one follows graph. navigation between pages should exist") | Today the app opens on **Graph**, and the sidebar is the only way between pages (six rows, ⌘1–6, from **G68**). Two open questions the user raised, neither decided: **(a) what should the front door be?** The argument for the **status/mascot page**: it answers "how is my memory doing right now" — sleep debt, what is queued, what needs attention — which is the question a person actually has on opening the app, and the graph is beautiful but is not a status. The argument for **Graph**: it is the product's identity and its most legible artifact, and demoting it hides the thing that makes Cicada feel different. A resolution worth considering: the front door is the *status* page and the graph is one tap away — or the two are adjacent and the app remembers where you were, which the tab-restore machinery (`AppTab.restored(from:)`) already supports. **(b) navigation between pages should exist** beyond the sidebar — the user's phrasing suggests sequential movement (prev/next), which today has no affordance: there are no keyboard arrows between tabs, no back/forward history across pages, and drilling into an entity from the graph and back is the only in-app "navigation" that exists. Worth deciding whether the model is **linear** (prev/next through the six pages), **historical** (browser-style back/forward across everything you visited, which fits the entity/conversation cross-linking of **G106**), or both. **Note the interaction with G106**: once entities link to conversations and back, a *history* stack becomes much more valuable than tab-order navigation, because the user will be following links rather than walking tabs — so deciding (b) before G106 lands risks building the wrong model. **Not yet decided; do not build either half without a ruling.** → touches **G68** (the six-row IA), the Sleep/status screen (**G107**), and **G106** (which makes cross-page history load-bearing). **Evidence for (b) from G115:** the inbox's `c` (open the raising conversation) / `e` (open the entity) / `⌘[` (back to the same card) loop is the first in-app navigation that follows a link rather than a tab, and it is history-shaped — a linear prev/next model would have nowhere to put "back to the question"; it does not bear on (a). | 🔲 |
| G109 | **🔴 URGENT — graph physics: deceleration is invisible and disconnected nodes explode into a ring** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, testing the shipped G84 fix: "the smooth deceleration after stopped holding a node doesnt happen and sometimes when i go back to the graph view all the nodes kind of explode… Isn't there opensource graphs we can base ourselves in for the physics and reactiveness? maybe obsidian?") | **Two distinct problems; the first is a tuning failure, the second is a regression I introduced.** **(1) Deceleration is implemented but tuned to be invisible.** `graph.js:809` sets `velocityDecay(0.45)` — each tick keeps only 55% of velocity, so a throw damps to 5% in **five ticks (~80 ms)** and reads as an instant stop. `alphaMin(0.05)` (`:810`) compounds it: d3's default is `0.001`, so the simulation *halts* far earlier than normal, and the release code's `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2))` buys only ~27 ticks before the sim stops entirely. The seeding logic added in G84(b) is correct — `seededDragVelocity` (`:1384`) and the smoothed per-move sampling (`:1447-1454`) do compute a real world-space velocity — but the damping constants swallow it. **A visible glide needs `velocityDecay` nearer 0.15-0.20 and `alphaMin` nearer d3's default**, which was deliberately avoided because of a documented instability at ~1,500 nodes — so this is a real tension, not an oversight, and it needs to be solved rather than re-tuned blindly. **(2) The "explosion" is disconnected nodes, and G84(a) caused it to be visible.** Fixing the cold-paint bug (`minDegree` 1 → 0) made every **zero-degree node render for the first time**. `forceManyBody` repels them, `forceCenter` is only `strength(0.04)` (`:825`), and with no links to pull them in they fly to the periphery and settle in the characteristic ring the user photographed — hundreds of pink (family-context) dots orbiting the connected core. So the nodes were always mis-laid-out; they were previously hidden. **d3-force has no good answer for disconnected nodes**: the honest options are a containment force (`forceX/forceY` toward the centre with real strength for degree-0 nodes — the `xType`/`yType` anchors at `:831-832` already exist and just need to bind these), a separate cluster/grid region for isolated nodes, or excluding them from the simulation and laying them out deterministically. **(3) The user's question deserves a real answer — evaluate alternatives rather than keep hand-tuning.** Candidates worth measuring at ~1,900 nodes / 6,800 links: **Obsidian's own approach** (Pixi.js WebGL renderer + custom forces — the closest analogue, and this project's own `CLAUDE.md` already cites it as the scale precedent), **cosmograph** (GPU/WebGL force layout, built for far larger graphs), **sigma.js + graphology** (ForceAtlas2, mature, worker-based), **ngraph.forcelayout**, and **d3-force-3d**. The decision axes: does it fix disconnected-node layout, does it give real momentum/settling, does it stay smooth at this scale, and what does it cost to port the existing behaviour (deltas keyed on `content_hash`, focus mode, facet rings, logo bitmaps, the observer dim, hub sizing). **Do not port blindly** — the current renderer carries a lot of earned behaviour. → fixes the remaining half of **G84**; interacts with **G84(c)(d)** (legend/colour work touches the same file); relates **G107** (the same "we hand-rolled art/physics that a library does better" question). **Ruling (2026-09-02, research run: inventory → five candidates → three judges → decision memo + critic):** keep d3-force and fix `graph.js` (judges 8.33/10; runner-up sigma.js + graphology + ForceAtlas2 at 5.64 — measured to contain isolates natively and 60 fps in a real WKWebView, but no velocity model, an esbuild step, and a 12–20-day port of the earned behaviours; Pixi is a renderer-only stage deferred behind the flip trigger; cosmos rebuilds velocity in the shader so a throw is impossible; ngraph's isolates are unbounded and `stable` never fires; d3-force-3d is byte-identical in 2D). The symptoms were three local bugs: (a) `hubGravityForce` ignored `alpha` — a permanent 5 %/tick spring against the never-alpha-scaled `forceCollide`, the real origin of the "1,500 nodes bounce forever" folklore that justified `velocityDecay` 0.45 / `alphaMin` 0.05 (bench: KE/node at tick 400 = 13–23 with alpha at 1e-9; 4e-6 once scaled); (b) the release path's `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2))` reheated the whole graph (~1,000 wu mean displacement of every other node) and let the link springs cancel a throw in one tick; (c) `forceCenter` is a uniform translation and cannot pull a node, so nothing opposed charge on degree-0 nodes. **Flip trigger:** p95 tick+draw > 16.7 ms on the live bank after phases 1–2 with isolates excluded (phase 3), or > ~10k nodes. **Phase 1 shipped (PR #32)** — `graph.js` only, no Swift, no dependency: hub gravity alpha-scaled (nominal 0.05 kept, id map hoisted into `initialize`); release `alphaTarget(0).restart()` with no bump; hold `alphaTarget(0.1)`; `alphaMin` 0.001; `velocityDecay` 0.2; `alphaDecay` 0.05 and `distanceMax` 700 unchanged; each isolate (`!hub && !memberToHub && !neighborsById` — visible degree 0 from `visibleLinks`, orphan facets included) owns a stable per-type free-list slot on a phyllotaxis disc (c = 20) centred on its type anchor, pulled by the existing `forceX/forceY` at 0.3 and exerting −30 charge; a last-registered `clampSpeed` at 60 wu/tick. Measured through `graph.js` itself on synthetic 300 / 1,500 / 1,500-dense graphs (`Tests/graph/graph-physics.bench.js`, real d3): KE/node@400 13 / 20 / 23 → 4e-16 / 4e-6 / 4e-5; a 28 wu/tick flick from rest 0 → 12–13 ticks, 0 → 63 / 99 / 100 wu; other-node displacement over the second after a release 979 / 1,194 / 1,275 → 1.8 / 8.8 / 28 wu; isolate max radius 2.0× (after the hub-gravity fix alone; on dev the metric is masked by the core itself sitting at r≈2,000) → 1.3× core p90 on the 1,500-node graphs and never beyond 1.1× the farthest connected node; core median within 4 %; ms/tick unchanged (~6.5–6.9). Superseded memo values: isolate pull 0.10 (measured to leave the ring at 1.6×; the anchor scale 1.0–1.8 does not move the outcome because per-node charge sets what a body *exerts*, not what it receives), and the "≤ 1.2× core p90" / "no isolate beyond 700 wu" criteria (geometrically incompatible with a disc beside its type cluster; replaced by the four asserted above). Disclosed: reheats run longer at `alphaMin` 0.001 (cold 59 → 135 ticks, a 0.3 reheat 35 → 112) and the cold layout still freezes at the alpha cut-off while relaxing; a new isolate from a delta seeds at its type anchor ±30 and transits ~350 wu to the disc's resting radius (the disc rests ~300 wu outward of the anchor it is centred on, so the newcomer crosses its type cluster over ~112 ticks — phase 3 seeds at the slot); and **a no-op delta — the app's most frequent reheat, every SSE version tick after a Sleep — still shuffles a packed core** (bench `deltaNoop*`, final review M1): core mean / max 8.8 / 49, 31 / 173, 80 / 573 wu on small / medium / dense (was 1,289 / 1,480 / 1,723 mean at `b690b66`; the test bounds it at < 200 mean), facets the top movers (one facet accumulates 1,082 wu over three deltas), isolate discs not the source (max 155). The delta reheat 0.3 → 0.1 is a real lever on medium density (core mean / max 31 / 173 → 19 / 60) but not on dense (80 / 573 → 73 / 340; ~65 wu mean even at 0.05): the residual is the never-alpha-scaled `forceCollide` re-resolving a core the alpha-scaled forces re-compress on every reheat, and the live bank (3.6 links/node) is denser than the dense synthetic (3.0). Left at 0.3 by R8/R10 — a tuning outcome, not a phase-1 fix. **Open — phase 2** (own the rAF loop: physical settle criterion, one tick per frame, `__cicadaPerf.report()` with tick/draw p50/p95 and the isolate/core radii, then the live-bank tuning pass over `ISOLATE_ANCHOR_STRENGTH` 0.2–0.5 / spacing 12–20 / hold 0.05–0.1 / vd 0.15–0.2 / **delta reheat 0.3 → 0.1 plus a collide lever (`iterations`/strength, or collide off below an alpha floor) for the no-op-delta shuffle** / `clearFocus` 0.2 → 0.1, the largest remaining whole-graph reheat on one gesture); **phase 3** (exclude isolates from the simulation: tick 6.7 → 4.5 ms measured, containment by construction, `pickNode` fallback scan); and the **Swift track** — `ContentView.swift:137-139` rebuilds the `WKWebView` on every tab switch, which is where "explosion on return" comes from (keep one web view alive; reset `isGraphReady` on teardown). | 🛠️ **phase 1 shipped (PR #32); phases 2–3 + Swift track open** |
| G110 | **Forking and handing off a live conversation — literal transcript transfer vs. memory handoff** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "an ability to include in cicada (forking ongoing conversations and such...)") | **The trigger:** Superset ships a button that transfers a whole Claude Code thread to "Codex, OpenCode, Pi, or any other agent", plus a separate "Fork session…". The stated pain is billing, not memory — hit a model's usage limit mid-session and be forced to downgrade or restart the work elsewhere. **Second prior-art data point, NOT yet researched — Cursor ships "Import from Claude Code"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, screenshot; flagged explicitly as "research how it works later"). Three things are visible in the dialog and each one matters to this row. **(i) It is a bulk *sync*, not a live handoff** — the button says `Sync`, it enumerates what it found (`Chats (2)`), and the copy is "Bring over your chats, and send a follow-up to fork it", i.e. **import-then-continue**: the thread is replayed into a new Cursor thread and forking happens by *sending the next message*, not by branching a live session. That is a materially different mechanism from Superset's per-thread transfer button and from `--fork-session`, and it is the cheapest of the three to implement — which is itself evidence about where the real difficulty is. **(ii) It necessarily reads the Claude Code transcript store** (`~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`), so it is a live existence proof that the read is practical — and a direct instance of the rail this row is bounded by: reading is fine, *landing content in a bank* is not. **(iii) The part neither Superset nor this row had considered: it syncs `Plugins & skills (12)` by default, alongside the chats.** A conversation is not self-contained — it presupposes the tools, skills and plugins that were available when it happened, and a thread replayed into an agent lacking them produces confident nonsense about capabilities it does not have. For Cicada this opens a **third packet dimension beyond conversation content and consolidated memory: capability context** — which is, notably, the one dimension Cicada is worst placed to supply and the harnesses are best placed to, since Cicada does not model the receiving agent's toolset at all. **Research questions for later, none answered here:** does Cursor read the transcript files directly or drive a Claude Code CLI/export path; does "send a follow-up to fork it" preserve any lineage id or start a genuinely fresh thread; what is the plugins/skills sync actually copying (files, a manifest, a marketplace reference); and does any of it survive when the source session is still live. Settle these before assuming Cicada should build any of the three modes — a mechanism two vendors have independently chosen is a stronger signal than either one alone. **Cicada is unusually well placed for the memory half, because G48 already shipped the hard part:** a conversation has an identity (`resolve_session_identity`, `mcp/server.py:62-89`), it is stamped onto every episode (`_session_frontmatter`, `:96-108`), it is credited transitively to entities through `source_episodes` (`session_stats._group`), it is written into git as `Cicada-Session:` trailers (`git_service.py:35-62`, capped 50), it is listed by `/conversations/recent` and reopened by `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` (`conversations.py:164-207`). Cicada already knows what a conversation *is* and what it *produced*. What it has never had is any relation **between** conversations. **The design fork, and it must be picked before anything is built. (a) Literal transfer** needs one reader per harness and there is no shared format: Claude Code transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<uuid>.jsonl`) are a parent/child-UUID DAG of Anthropic-Messages turns (`content[].type == "text"`), Codex rollouts (`~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`) are a flat sequential log of OpenAI-Responses turns (`content[].type == "input_text"`) carrying `sandbox_policy`/`permission_profile`/git metadata with no Claude analogue — and the codebase's own precedent (`conversations.py:213` `detect_source()` dispatching to one `parse_*` per platform) already answers this: **one translator per harness, not one interchange format**. Both vendors also already ship the primitive (`claude --resume` / `--fork-session`; `codex resume [SESSION_ID|thread_name]` / `codex fork [SESSION_ID]`, both defaulting to a cwd-filtered picker), so a literal transfer is Cicada re-implementing two CLIs' internals cross-vendor. **State the rail precisely, because the loose version is false:** it is *not* "no code opens a session log" — `harness_stats.codex_rate_limits` (`api/services/harness_stats.py:63-88`, live behind `GET /consumption/harness`, `routers/consumption.py:112`) already `read_text()`s and `json.loads()`es whole Codex rollout files today. The rail is **no transcript content enters a bank**, and G76 measured why: 741 MB `~/.claude/projects` and 73 MB `~/.codex/sessions` against a 2.4 MB curated bank. A translator that lands turns in `memory/` crosses it; one that pipes them CLI-to-CLI does not. **(b) Memory handoff** — mint the child its own identity and seed it with a small packet assembled from what the parent already got consolidated: the entity ids touched (`session_stats.find_conversation`, read **uncapped** — the summary projection truncates at `MAX_CONVERSATION_ENTITIES=12`, `session_stats.py:40`), the claims it wrote (`parse_claims`, `claims.py:175`, filtered to claims whose `source_episodes` intersect this conversation's episodes or whose `all_session_ids()` contains it), and the open questions on those entities — the last an **approximation only**, since inbox items key on `entity_id` and carry no session id anywhere (`inbox_service.py:81,333-334,636-638`; `InboxItem`, `schemas.py:795-819`). **(c) Continue from compact — Rodrigo's third framing, and it reorders the other two** (2026-09-01: "we could have the option to continue from compact (handoff kind of), on another agent (could be another harness), or continue with the same stream (which is what fork is probably)"). **First, a vocabulary correction that swaps which mode is hard:** in Claude Code's own terms `--fork-session` branches to a **new** id, while plain `--resume` continues the **same** id — so "continue with the same stream" is *not* fork, it is resume, and `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` **already does it today**; true forking is a one-flag change. **Second, the compaction boundary is a real and better-shaped seam than a transcript read, but the hook Cicada has written down is the wrong one.** `PreCompact` fires *before* the summary exists — verified structurally against a live transcript: the `compact_boundary` event carries only metadata (`preTokens`/`postTokens`/`trigger`, measured 905,931 → 26,812 tokens, ~30x) and the ~12.6-18.2k-char prose summary is the *next* line, a synthetic `user` turn. The hook that carries content is **`PostCompact`**, which is real and **appears nowhere in Cicada's docs or backlog**. **Third, and this is the trap:** G49 does list a "PreCompact flush" and G76 specs that hook precisely — as a **content-free pointer** (`{session_id, transcript_path, cwd, reason}`, "a pointer, NO content"). So carrying a compaction summary is **not** a second consumer of an already-designed capture point; it deliberately cuts against what G76 chose, and needs its own ruling rather than being smuggled in as an extension. No hooks are installed in the repo today at all (no `hooks` key in `.claude/settings.json`). **Why the framing is still the strongest of the three:** a compaction summary is already lossy, already prose, already stripped of harness structure — i.e. already the packet shape (b) has to construct — and it exists at exactly the moment the user hits the limit the tweet complains about, which is the one moment (b)'s packet is emptiest. **The tempting parallel, checked and only half-true:** a harness compacting because its window filled is structurally the same operation as Sleep — episodic turns compressed into semantic summary under capacity pressure — and Cicada's provenance/claims apparatus is a genuine edge over bare summary prose. But it does **not** cash out as a shortcut: Sleep is nightly batch over *closed* episodes while a hook has seconds mid-task, and compaction content is mid-task working state — the exact granularity G76 already excluded as "volume without judgment". The parallel justifies caring, not reusing the pipeline. **Fourth objection, specific to this half:** the hook surface is a moving target — the live hooks doc lists **33 event names**, most (`Setup`, `PostToolBatch`, `WorktreeCreate`, `PreModelSwitch`, …) absent from Cicada's own inventory — so anything built tightly against it dates fast. **What it does to provenance.** No conversation-to-conversation relation exists: zero hits for `parent_session`/`forked_from`/`thread_id` across every `*.py` and `*.swift`; `ConversationSummary` (`schemas.py:263-296`) and its Swift twin carry a flat `conversation_id`, a closed `kind ∈ {mcp, import}`, and the entities touched — nothing pointing at another conversation. An optional `parent_session_id` threads through exactly the four places `session_id` already flows (episode frontmatter, the 3-field frozen `SessionIdentity` at `mcp/server.py:55-59`, `_group`/`project_conversation`'s "first non-empty across the group" collection, one optional field on `ConversationSummary` + its Swift twin); trailers need no change (already a dedup'd flat list) but **degrade semantically** — a cycle spanning a parent and its forks reads as N unrelated conversations. **Memory stays shared, never branched:** the only branch-memory primitive is `POST /banks/{name}/duplicate`, the wrong grain (a deliberate life-partition, not a per-session artifact), and a working tree per fork would force all 20 routers to become branch-aware and break filesystem-as-single-source-of-truth. **One thing to NOT claim:** sibling forks that contradict each other are not silently archived — `claim_reconciler.trust_decision` (`claim_reconciler.py:170-236`) is trust-gated, returns `SUPERSEDE` for two `agent_extracted` claims only `if newer` and otherwise `CONFLICT_NUDGE` (`:210-212`), and since `valid_from` is always a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` two same-day forks tie and **already raise an inbox card**. The narrow real gap is that the card's option description (`_claim_option`, `:248-259` — age plus source episode) cannot say "this was a deliberate branch point". **Strongest objection, and it is serious: the packet is emptiest exactly when it is wanted.** Sleep is batch by ruling ("Batch over real-time consolidation"), and `_get_unprocessed_episodes` / `POST /sleep/trigger` run over the whole bank's `processed: false` backlog — there is no scoped-to-one-conversation sleep — so a session forked twenty minutes in has episodes and near-zero consolidated claims, and populating its packet would mean a synchronous bank-wide Sleep (slow, real spend, touches unrelated entities). The feature's value therefore skews to forking **old, already-slept** conversations, not the live "I hit my limit, hand off now" case the tweet is actually about.
