Second objection: a brand-new session already inherits everything Sleep consolidated, for free, via `cicada_recall` ("use this at the start of conversations", `mcp/server.py:137`) — lineage metadata improves provenance *presentation*, it unblocks no capability. **NOT DECIDED — do not build a half on assumption.** (i) literal mid-session branch vs. linear handoff (Superset ships both as separate buttons; only the handoff half is satisfiable inside the bank rail); (ii) whether a same-harness fork should get a NEW session id at all, or keep the parent's — keeping it sidesteps **G104** entirely for the common case, while a fork otherwise makes G104 strictly worse (three fragments of one shared prefix, and the "2+ separate conversations" promotion threshold reading siblings as independent corroboration); (iii) who calls the packet — a new MCP tool the parent invokes pre-handoff, or the user pasting; (iv) whether cross-harness lineage is admissible at all, since no signal crosses harnesses (a Codex rollout has no Claude UUID concept), so `parent_session_id` would be a **declared** fact self-reported by a third-party tool — an unauthenticated provenance channel Cicada has never accepted; (v) **UNVERIFIED** whether `claude --fork-session` exposes a parent-session env var to the child's MCP process, and under what name — only a structurally analogous pair was observed live (`CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` beside `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1`, for subagent spawns), and `resolve_session_identity` reads neither; settle it with an actual `--fork-session` env dump. Note that a *minting* path is not the gap — tier 2 is documented as an "explicit override for any MCP client" with `CICADA_SESSION_HARNESS` naming the harness (`mcp/server.py:42-43`) and tier 3 mints a `ses_` id for any caller (`:82-86`); what is missing is a Codex-side auto-detect branch. **Triage: RESEARCH, not committed work — and the cheap half is already filed.** G53's state dictionary plus G75's handshake hand any connecting agent a curated cursor with no transcript read at all; a fork packet is largely that payload plus a `from_conversation_id`, so build those first and see whether the fork want survives. → extends **G48** (conversation identity, the only reason this is addressable at all), reuses **G53**/**G75** rather than duplicating them; bounded by **G76**'s no-transcript-content-in-a-bank rail and its 741 MB / 73 MB / 2.4 MB measurement, and **contradicting G76's content-free-pointer choice** for the compact half, which needs its own ruling; extends **G49** (whose plugin design names a PreCompact flush but not `PostCompact`, the hook this actually needs); blocked on a **G104** ruling (a fork multiplies its double-consolidation harm); relates **G105** (the per-harness transcript stores and the absence of an interchange format) and **G106** (the two-way conversation↔entity loop a packet would render). | 🔲 |
| G111 | **Ingest newsletters (TLDR, TLDR AI) and answer "what landed today that matters to me"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "process emails from newsletters like tldr ai, and tldr… so i could ask my agent about relevant things from today that might be relevant to me and my research") | **Read the ask precisely: the ingestion is the easy half and it is not what was asked for.** The request is a *question* — "relevant to me and my research" — so the deliverable is a **relevance filter over a firehose**, not another capture channel. Cicada is well placed for exactly that and badly placed for nothing else here: the graph already encodes what the user works on and cares about, so scoring an incoming item against existing entities/claims is the differentiator. Raw ingestion alone produces a second inbox the user will not read. **Do the cheap path first and it may be the whole path.** TLDR publishes web archives and per-edition RSS — **VERIFIED 2026-09-01 by direct probe:** `https://tldr.tech/api/rss/ai` (TLDR AI) and `https://tldr.tech/rss` / `/api/rss/tech` (TLDR) are live RSS 2.0 feeds, 20 items each, **one item per edition** (`<link>` = `https://tldr.tech/ai/YYYY-MM-DD`, `<guid isPermaLink>`, `<pubDate>`, **no `<description>`/`content:encoded`** — the feed carries only the edition headline), and each edition page is **static server-rendered HTML** (~75 KB, no `__NEXT_DATA__`, JSON-LD present) holding **19 `<article>` blocks** per edition — one per story, each a headline suffixed "(N minute read)", a 2-4 sentence summary and exactly one outbound `href` with `utm_source=tldrai` — plus a sponsor `<article>` with a `(Sponsor)` suffix and `doubleclick` tracking links that a parser must drop. So the mailbox is never needed: the feed gives the edition stream, and the per-story split G111 needs is a ~40-line `<article>` splitter over the edition page, with the story's canonical URL (utm stripped) as the item id. No mailbox access is needed at all, because `feed_registry.subscribe_feed` (`api/services/feed_registry.py:80`) plus the existing `POST /sources/poll-feeds` and the Sleep-tail poll already do the whole capture job with **zero new consolidation code** — the module's own docstring says a poll is "identical to a manual `POST /sources/rss` paste". Verify the archive/RSS surface before writing a single line of email code. **Email is a categorically larger surface than RSS and must not be adopted casually.** An IMAP/Gmail connector reaches *everything* in the mailbox, not the newsletters — a scope the secrets posture (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, 0600) can hold but the *blast radius* of a bug cannot. If email is genuinely required, the only two acceptable shapes are (i) a **dedicated forwarding address / label-scoped** read (`label:tldr`), never a whole-mailbox grant, or (ii) the user forwarding issues by hand. Either way it is a peer adapter under `api/services/connectors/` honouring the documented `ADAPTERS` contract (`connectors/__init__.py:35,69`) — `base.run_sync` already owns the not-connected skip, the `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` background gate, error/skip recording and the chunked `ingest_batch`; do not hand-roll a second polling loop. **The parsing unit is the ITEM, not the email — this is the structural point.** One TLDR AI issue is ~8-10 *unrelated* stories, each a link plus a written summary; ingesting the email as one media item produces a page about nothing. It is the same one-source-many-items shape G71 already solved for saved-content exports, and it should reuse that machinery: a parser emitting a per-item breakdown, previewable through the identical `?preview=true` path before anything is staged. **The trap, and it is in the code today: these must NOT be evergreen.** `decay_policy.INGEST_SOURCES` (`api/services/decay_policy.py:49`) is `{media, bookmark, rss, pdf, ingest}` and everything on those paths is evergreen — decay-exempt, never archived, no decay nudge — with the stated rationale that *"a saved bookmark does not become less true by going unmentioned"*. That reasoning is sound because **saving is a deliberate act**. A newsletter arrives unbidden: the user chose the subscription, never the item. Routing 10 items/day through the `rss` path at evergreen would permanently inflate the graph with things the user never selected and would quietly break "absence of mention is a signal" — the one mechanism that keeps the graph honest. **So newsletter items need a decaying class (likely `volatile`, since news is stale in weeks) and therefore either a new writer-source tag outside `INGEST_SOURCES` or an explicit per-write override.** Decide this BEFORE the first ingest run, because retro-fixing decay classes across thousands of pages needs another one-shot migration. **Promotion is the natural filter, not a new mechanism:** an item mentioned once stays a chunk in the vector index; an item whose topic the user actually discusses crosses the existing promotion threshold on its own. That is the entity-promotion model doing precisely the job it was designed for, applied to a feed instead of a conversation — so the anti-pollution answer is mostly *already built*, provided the decay class is right. **What is genuinely new:** a daily-digest surface answering "what landed today that touches what I am working on", which is `POST /ask` (**G52**) pointed at a time-boxed slice and ranked against the graph rather than a free-text question — and worth checking whether it belongs in the Feed page or as a Sleep-time nudge rather than yet another screen. **Open:** whether unread/unscored items should ever reach the graph at all, or live only in the index until something promotes them. → extends **G71** (the connector adapter contract + the per-collection preview shape) and the shipped RSS/feeds half; bounded by **G66** (decay classes — the evergreen trap above is the binding constraint); serves **G52** (Ask is the surface the request actually names); relates **G105** (deterministic, non-agent-judgment capture — a newsletter parser is the same argument) and **G101** (what raw content is retained vs consolidated). | 🔲 |
| G112 | **Portable skills — compile procedural memory into SKILL.md bundles anyone can load on their own plan** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, on WikiSkill: "relevant to the capture of skill learning and make it as portable as possible so I can have other people use it on their own with their ai plans and stuff"; the Era-of-Experience framing now in CLAUDE.md) | **What exists today (verified 2026-09-01, five-agent grounding sweep, critic spot-checked):** `skill` is producible by Stage 1 (`schemas.py:45-56`; `entity_extractor.py:56,:72,:96`) and by Stage 4 (`skill_extractor.detect_patterns` :57-101 — one LLM call over this cycle's changes + pairs co-occurring in ≥3 episodes, output `{skills:[{name, description, evidence_entities, confidence}]}`), and its only metric is a count (`skills_detected`, `schemas.py:946` → the Sleep-view chip). **The Stage-4 writer is create-only and ungrounded:** `inbox_generator.py:248-270` writes a page only `if not skill_path.exists()` (:254), dedups on `sanitize_id(name)` alone (:252), stamps `source_episodes: []` / `tags: []` / `related: []` (:265-267), body = the raw description (:270), and DISCARDS `evidence_entities`. Stage-4 output bypasses `entity_resolver` and conflict detection entirely (`sleep_cycle.py:781-786,:819-824`), so a re-detected pattern is a silent no-op that never bumps `last_referenced`, a near-duplicate name makes a second page, a contradicting preference is never a conflict, and the page only ever moves via durable decay (`conflict_resolver.py:146-232`) into the generic "No recent mentions" nudge. Contrast the Stage-1 branch (`conflict_resolver.py:257-317`), which stamps episodes, composes a v2 body and merges on update; `source_rewrite` cannot re-ground a skill because its `source_episodes` is empty. **Nothing exports skills:** the only installable skills are two hand-written files (root `SKILL.md` = recall policy; `skills/cicada-librarian/SKILL.md` = consolidation loop) — neither is generated from memory, `install.sh --skill` (:339-347) and `ConnectView.swift:75-76` copy only the root one, nothing installs `cicada-librarian`. `_procedures/` and `_preferences.md` are scaffolded in every bank (`bank_registry.py:45-55,:228-234`) and read by nothing; MCP `initialize` carries no `instructions` (`mcp/server.py:413-431`); `cicada_write_claim` cannot create a `skill` page (`agentic_write._infer_entity_type` :48-79 has no skill signal). `context-passport-roadmap.md:22` rates Skills "✅ Strong" — an overstatement; the honest rating is *captured, not grounded, not exported* (corrected in that file). **Why this is not G72:** G72 is the inventory + lifecycle of skills the user already HAS on disk (WikiSkill's raw→wiki→skills loop, R2's gate, the negative-transfer finding). G112 is the third layer WikiSkill names and Cicada lacks: compiling what memory has learned INTO skills, and shipping them so someone else can load them on their own plan. **Design.** (1) **Ground the skill page first ($0):** route Stage-4 output through `entity_resolver` like Stage 1; on update merge `source_episodes` from the evidence episodes and bump `last_referenced`; write `evidence_entities` into `related` (and edges); give the page the v2 layout so `source_rewrite` and hubs work on it; let a Stage-4 preference that contradicts an existing skill page raise a normal conflict item. (2) **Compile, don't author:** a deterministic `skill_compiler` turns a `skill` entity into a SKILL.md bundle — frontmatter `name`/`description` written as a TRIGGER block (G76's finding: the description is what a harness matches on), body = the preference/procedure text plus a `## Evidence` list of the claim ids it rests on with their `observer`/`source_trust`, and a `Cicada-Origin:` line naming the bank commit it was compiled from — provenance only, never a runtime dependency. WikiSkill's executor-wiki-access result (−2.8) is the rule: the compiled skill must stand alone on a harness that has never heard of Cicada. (3) **Redact by construction:** a compiled skill carries the RULE, never the episodes — no `source_episodes`, no conversation text, no third-party names unless the user pins them; `## Evidence` cites claim ids, not quotes. That is what makes "other people use it" safe: a skill bundle is shareable where a bank is not. (4) **Surfaces:** `GET /skills/{id}/export` (one bundle) and `GET /skills/export` (zip of every `active` skill with confidence ≥ 0.6); `install.sh --skill` and the Connect page install the compiled bundles beside the two hand-written ones; per-harness targets are the three the ecosystem converged on — `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, `~/.codex/skills/`, and an `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` block for harnesses without a skills dir. (5) The always-on primer half (top-N preferences <200 tokens at conversation start, `docs/goals/d2-research/procedural-preference-memory.md:9-34`) is NOT this row — it is G75's `initialize.instructions` payload, and it should read the same compiled bundles rather than a second format. **Rails:** a compiled skill is proposed, never pushed (R2's gate; G72's negative-transfer finding); the compiler is deterministic and free; Stage-4 grounding is the one paid change and it reuses the existing call. **Sequencing:** (1) is a bug fix and ships first; (2)+(3)+(4) are one PR; (5) waits for G75. → completes the WikiSkill mapping in **G72** (that row = inventory of existing skills; this = compiling new ones) and the SKILL.md-export line of **G55** (executable procedures ride the same compiler); serves **G76** (install both skills + the compiled ones), **G75** (primer payload), **G92** (a shareable skill bundle is the first zero-bank onboarding artifact), **A4** (what "enrich skill capture" should have meant); consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G66** (durable class), `entity_body` v2, `source_rewrite`; bounded by **R2** (human gate) and **G48**'s no-transcript-content rail. | 🔲 |
| G113 | **Grounded reward — record every human verdict on memory so extraction quality becomes a number** (Silver & Sutton, *Welcome to the Era of Experience*: a reward must be "grounded" in the environment's reply rather than in a designer's prior judgement; Rodrigo 2026-09-01 shared the paper as Cicada's framing) | **The finding (verified 2026-09-01):** the user grades the extractor dozens of times — every decay keep/archive, conflict pick/both/neither, clarification answer, merge accept/reject — and NOTHING records the verdict as a signal. `inbox_service.resolve` commits `trigger: inbox/{kind}/resolved` whatever the action (:295-297) — keep and archive produce byte-identical manifest lines; the chosen option survives only as a diffable inbox-file deletion. No telemetry event is written on any resolution (`telemetry.py:21` KINDS has no resolution kind; `inbox_service.py` never imports it; `kind="ask"`/`"import"` are declared and never emitted). `GET /consumption/*` and `GET /contributors` count cost and volume, never agreement (`consumption_stats.py:91-252`; `git_service.py:645-745`). Extractor confidence (`entity_extractor.py:28-47`; relationship default 0.6 :514; "<0.5" gates clarification :106) is never compared with an outcome. `claim_reconciler._reinforce` (:143-162) bumps confidence with no count, so `session_ids` (`claims.py:97-106`) is the only precision proxy. Stage-3's `audit` list — the one machine record of "an agent claim was overruled by the trust table" — is returned by `claim_pipeline` (:159) and read by nobody (`grep audit sleep_cycle.py` → 0). **Four verdicts are actively lost:** (a) the `divergence` ("Keep my statement / Update to X") and `normalization` ("Correct fold / Wrong fold") items Stage 5.56 writes (`inbox_generator.py:333-338`) are NOT in `InboxKind` (`schemas.py:765-769`) — `InboxItem(kind='divergence')` raises ValidationError (verified), `load_inbox` skips them as unparseable (:169-172), resolve would 400 (:286-287) — so the two questions that most directly grade the extractor accumulate on disk unanswerable; (b) merge REJECT has no action — the app sends `dismiss`, which deletes the item (`inbox_service.py:712-713`; `InboxCardView.swift:177`), and `clarification_manager.find_open` (:73-83) re-proposes the same pair next cycle; (c) decay `keep_active` never touches the claim the nudge came from (`claim_id` written at `inbox_generator.py:365`, never read in `_resolve_decay` :331-368) and `remind_later` writes a `snooze_until` nothing reads (:354-355) while still committing "entity updated"; (d) a clarification answer writes body prose only (:667-710) — no `user_stated` claim, so it earns none of the human protection a conflict "neither" earns (:549-574). Also: `dropped` status is read in five places and set by none outside a test fixture; an out-of-band editor correction is swept into the next Sleep commit under `sleep/extraction` with the MODEL's author trailer (`sleep_cycle.py:1462-1479,:1553-1569`) — the `user/manual_edit` trigger in CLAUDE.md is emitted by no production code; `dedup_sweep` verdicts (:82-99) are returned, never persisted, and re-judged (paid) every sweep. **Why this matters beyond bookkeeping:** the paper's argument is that an agent cannot improve past its designer's priors without a reward grounded in consequences. Cicada already HAS the grounded signal — the user's verdicts are the environment's reply — and throws it away, which is why G78 calls measurement "the weakest link" and why G98's 22/49-dead-subjects number came from a manual sweep. **Design — a feedback ledger, $0.** (1) **Carry the verdict in the commit:** trigger `inbox/{kind}/resolved:{action}` (`keep_active`, `archive`, `pick:<key>`, `both`, `neither`, `answer`, `merge`, `reject`, `defer`) — inert to entity-line parsing like every trailer; `_infer_change_type` learns `archived`. (2) **Emit a telemetry event** `kind="resolution"` with refs `{item_id, kind, predicate, entity_id, action, option_key, winner_claim_id, loser_claim_ids, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, item_age_days}` from `inbox_service.resolve` (the MCP `cicada_resolve_inbox` path only POSTs, so one writer covers both); plus `kind="audit"` for Stage-3's `audit` list and `kind="dedup_verdict"` for `dedup_sweep`. (3) **Make the four lost verdicts real:** add `divergence` + `normalization` to `InboxKind` with resolve branches (keep-mine / update / both; correct / wrong-fold → unfold + record the pair); a `reject` action on merge that writes the pair to `_merge_rejected.yaml`, consulted by `find_open` and `dedup_sweep` before proposing; decay keep restores the source claim's confidence; clarification answers write a `user_stated` claim (`origin: clarification`) beside the prose. (4) **Read it back:** `GET /consumption/feedback` — per-model / per-predicate / per-kind agreement rates (keep vs archive, pick-agent vs neither, correct vs wrong fold), a calibration curve (extractor-confidence bucket → user-agreement rate), and a corrections count from `Cicada-Author: user` commits — rendered as a fourth card on Activity. (5) **Close the loop later (💸, DECIDE):** feeding rates back into prompts or thresholds is G78's territory and needs the ledger first; the ledger is the prerequisite, not the optimizer. **Rails:** the ledger lives in `~/.cicada/telemetry/` like every other event — machine-global, never in the bank, ids and enums only, no personal text; a verdict is recorded, never auto-applied to future extraction without the user seeing the rate. → the measurement half of **G78** (gbrain-evals) and a live number for **G98**; extends **G51** (ledger + `/consumption`), **G60** (claim-aware resolve), **G85** (author-attribution correctness is the precondition); consumes **G97** (context on the question side); relates **G103** (observer in the UI), **G100** (span citation as the thing a user confirms or denies), **G72**/**G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` is only trustworthy once verdicts are counted). **(2b, decided in G115, 2026-09-02; slices 1–2 merged as PR #31 at `09a4b66`, `_verdict` at `inbox_service.py:302`, `remind_later → _defer(7)` still Task 5):** the wire carries the verdict before the answer — each option gets `recommended` (the one option `_verdict` scores `agreed`: Sleep's proposal, never freshness, so a stale-escalated `neither` and a no-claim entity-path conflict never carry it) and `verdict`, computed once from R3 so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `merge_suggestion` never carries `recommended` (G81); the resolve response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces show only `changed`; the shipped `resolution` event gains additive refs `surface (app|mcp|telegram)`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`, and a new `ask` kind (ids only) records each item an agent surfaced so asked-vs-answered is a number; `session_id` lives in the ledger, not in a `Cicada-Session:` trailer on the user commit (contract unchanged pending G116). | 🔲 |
| G114 | **Capture-writer hygiene — one id rule, one timestamp shape, one unattended poll** (grounding sweep 2026-09-01, verified) | Five defects across the seven episode writers, all $0 and deterministic. (1) **Importer id collision:** `conversations.py:743-746,:820-823` numbers episodes by COUNTING existing `ep_*.md` per date — the scheme `mcp/server.py:1690`'s own comment says "collides and overwrites" — and `markdown_parser.write` (:850) overwrites on collision, so any gap in a day's sequence (one deleted file) makes the next import silently replace a real episode. MCP, media, Telegram, calendar and notes all use max-suffix+1 (`media_ingestor.py:1322-1333`; `telegram_capture.py:398-404`). Extract one `next_episode_id(episodes_dir, date)` and use it everywhere. (2) **Timestamp shape:** MCP and Telegram write aware UTC (`server.py:1710`; `telegram_capture.py:408`); media, calendar, notes and the ChatGPT importer write NAIVE LOCAL time with a literal `Z` appended (`media_ingestor.py:1389-1392`; `calendar_registry.py:346`; `notes_sync.py:231`; `conversations.py:406/418/429`) — mislabelled as UTC by up to a timezone offset; Sleep's ordering is a string sort over the mix (`sleep_cycle.py:1126`); `claude_memory` imports carry `timestamp: None` and sort first (:305-338); `sleep_debt.py:108-111`'s docstring still claims MCP writes naive local. One `episode_timestamp()` helper, aware UTC; no migration — within-day ordering is the only consumer. (3) **Telegram:** `/remind` is matched by `_COMMAND_RE` (:48) and has no handler; the message `date` is parsed (:129-133) and never written (:216), so a Telegram episode's timestamp is receipt time — pass it through, and either implement `/remind` as a `remind_after` inbox item or drop it from the regex. (4) **Unattended polling:** RSS, calendar, Apple Notes and bookmarks are button-only (`sources.py:517-608`; the app's only `Timer`s are animations); only the three connectors poll at Sleep's tail (`_poll_connectors_safely` :277-323). Add feeds + calendars to `_run_engine_independent_tail` under the same non-fatal-slot pattern and the existing `CICADA_ALLOW_FEED_FETCH` gate — G111's newsletter feed is worthless if it refreshes only when a button is pressed. (5) `cicada_mark_processed` flips `processed: true` with no consolidation (`server.py:1162-1190` → `agentic_write.py:463`), so "unprocessed" ≠ "never consolidated" — stamp `processed_by: agent|sleep` so G104/G105 can tell them apart. → unblocks **G111** (scheduled feed poll); serves **G105**/**G104** (a reliable timeline is what deterministic extraction sorts on) and **G101** (the raw layer needs stable ids); relates **G29** (Telegram). **Shipped 2026-09-01 (PR #30, `feat/capture-hygiene`, merged to dev):** `api/services/episode_ids.py` owns both rules every writer duplicated — `next_episode_id` (R1: max-suffix+1 per date; the importer seeds its per-batch dict from `max_suffix_by_date` instead of counting files) and `utc_now_iso`/`to_utc_iso` (R2: aware UTC, `+00:00`) — and importer, MCP, media, Telegram, calendar and notes all call it; Sleep orders its queue by `timestamp_sort_key`, which normalises naive-local and `Z`-suffixed legacy stamps to UTC. **R3:** a Telegram episode's timestamp (and its id's date part) is the message's own `date`, and for a URL save that date becomes `RawItem.added`, so a webhook retry or late delivery never restamps yesterday's message as today. **R4:** `/remind` stays in `_COMMAND_RE`, is saved as a note episode with `capture_kind: reminder`, and ACKs honestly ("Saved as a note — reminders aren't scheduled yet."); a real `remind_after` reminder is a feature for its own row, not a half-build here. **R5:** `_poll_feeds_and_calendars_safely` runs in `_run_engine_independent_tail` beside the connector poll, under the SAME clean-tree guard (both registries commit with `git add -A`) and the EXISTING opt-in `CICADA_ALLOW_FEED_FETCH=1`; `install.sh`'s LaunchAgent plist now sets it on a fresh install (it never rewrites a plist behind an already-running backend — an older plist needs the key by hand, TODO.md Live environment), the test suite never does; zero subscriptions logs nothing. **R6:** `processed_by` is written only beside `processed: true` — `sleep` from a Sleep cycle, `agent` (or the harness name) from `cicada_mark_processed` — never removed, and surfaced on `GET /sleep/episodes` as an optional field so an older app build keeps decoding. **Disclosed non-fix:** no migration of legacy timestamps — existing files keep whatever shape they were written with; every reader (`sleep_debt._parse_episode_timestamp`, `fromisoformat`, the sort key) tolerates all three shapes, and within-day ordering was the only consumer. | ✅ |
| G115 | **Inbox redesign — one question object, cause on the card, Sleep's proposal marked, a scoped "don't ask again", and an in-conversation ask gate** (decision memo 2026-09-02, four-design study judged on three lenses — UI-port spine 115 / stream-first 114 / conversation-first 109 / reward-first 109; a four-point spread is a mandate for a spine and grafts, not for one design; critic pass applied, base `dev @ 09a4b66`; owner's complaints in G97/G98/G100/G103/G113) | **Problem.** The inbox is a priority-sorted list of cards that hide their cause (G97), never say what Sleep is betting on, grade the person forever one item at a time with no way to write a ruling once, reach an agent only through a body-text substring match that returns everything (`mcp/server.py::_topic_matches`, no G98 subject gate, no kind check, a hand-rolled frontmatter parser at `:738` that disagrees with the API about which files exist), and make decay a three-button special case outside the one component G60 built (`InboxCardView.swift:162-164` short-circuits `actionRow` on `.decay`; `_resolve_decay`'s else-branch unlinks the file). **Evidence (verified).** G98: 45% of items unanswerable; G105: 0 MCP invocations in 12 days, so "agent-initiated" is theoretical; `InboxItem` (`schemas.py:795-819`) carries no `source_episode`, `entity_type` or recommendation; **the ETag trap has two halves** — `api/routers/inbox.py` must widen `etag_for(mp, "inbox", "entities", "episodes")` AND `VersionVector.swift:8-9` maps `entities` → `[.graph,.contributors,.origins]` and `episodes` → `[.status,.origins,.sources,.channels]`, neither refreshing `.inbox`, so `.inbox` must be added to both mappings in the same commit or `SnapshotCache` keeps stale context until the inbox itself moves; `graph_builder._dir_mtime` (`:536-544`) globs `*.md` + dir mtime, so a YAML rule file under `inbox/` would tick nothing; a per-cycle write cap is a silent drop for Stage-2 clarifications (`entity_resolver.pending_actions` on an episode then marked processed) — so **no cap**. **Design (the ruling).** (1) One question object for every kind — `decay`/`divergence`/`normalization` synthesised at read in `_item_from_file` (decay: `Still tracking {name}?`, options `archive`/`keep`, `allow_defer=True`), no file rewrite; clarification `title` becomes the question (`Who is {name}?`). (2) **Item shape, all additive** — `InboxOption += {recommended, verdict, action; Phase 2: observer, authored_by, confidence, episode_id, excerpt, preview}`; `InboxItem += {entity_type, source_episode, source_episode_timestamp, cause: {episode_id, timestamp, conversation_id, harness, origin, conversation_title, excerpt, mention_offsets, tier: item|claim|entity, span_kind: derived}, silent_days, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, recommended_key, group_id, defer_count; Phase 2: neighbours, rule_offers}`; `InboxResolveRequest += {surface: app|mcp|telegram, harness, session_id (ledger refs only), note, rule}`; response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces render only `changed`. `age_days`, `silent_days`, `cause.excerpt`, `mention_offsets`, `recommended`, `verdict` are derived at read, never stored. (3) `cause` resolves by G97's three tiers (item → freshest option claim → subject `source_episodes`), `[ no source recorded ]` served when all fail, never a hidden card; a `bank_index.parse_count` budget test guards the ~100 ms figure. (4) **`recommended` == the one option the shipped `_verdict` (`inbox_service.py:302`, R3) scores `agreed` — Sleep's proposal, never freshness** — computed once so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `verdict` is on the wire for agents and tests and never rendered as copy. Corollaries (critic C1/C0): on a stale-escalated conflict `Neither anymore` is `overruled` under R3, so it is NEVER marked — either nothing is marked or Sleep's own claim stays marked and freshness orders the rest; an entity-path conflict with no item `claim_id` grades every pick `neutral` (the Task-2 footnote) and therefore never carries a `(Recommended)` — a large share of live conflicts per G98. **Never on `merge_suggestion`, no initial highlight, never in a bundle (G81)**; `reject` persists to `_merge_rejected.yaml` (R5). (5) `<memory>/_inbox_rules.yaml` at bank root beside `_merge_rejected.yaml`, permission-rule grammar `kind(scope[, scope]): decision`, scope values enums/slugs only (parser rejects whitespace, so a rule string is ledger-safe), precedence `never > defer > action > ask`; Phase 2 ships `never` + `defer:N` + `decay(entity=X): never` → the existing evergreen `PUT`; merge rules are `never` only; a `rules` sync component (`file_mtime`) mapped to `[.inbox]`; rule EXECUTION (`archive|keep|accept`) waits on **G116(a)**. A `never` rule is NOT the tool for G98's entity-resolution artifacts (~15 of 27 conflicts) — that half stays G98's Stage-2 fix and must not be `never`-ruled away. (6) `cicada_check_nudges(mode="ask"|"list", entity_ids, topic, limit=3, kinds, conversation_id, include_context)` gated server-side by a pure `inbox_ask_gate.candidates(...)`: `entity_id ∈ entity_ids` OR a top-5 `search_entities` hit with score ≥ 0.55 (`vector_index.py:295`, `1.0 − distance`) OR a ≥4-char name token — body-only lexical matches dropped; every `load_inbox` gate (G98 subject gate, not deferred, `kind ∈ InboxKind`, no `never` rule); an in-process `_ASKED_THIS_SESSION` set (one MCP process = one conversation, G48 — a skip is never written to the bank); `normalization` hidden from the agent path; hard cap 5. Both MCP readers (`handle_check_nudges`, `_relevant_inbox`) go through `inbox_service.load_inbox`. `render_question` v2 prints `inbox-NNN · <kind> · entity_id=<slug>` (the slug is what the next call's `entity_ids=` needs), the question, a `Cause:` line, `k) label (Recommended) — <age> · <observer_label>`; the existing `a) Label — 6 months ago` prefix is preserved for `test_mcp_inbox_questions.py`. The discipline (one question per turn, after the work, quote the Cause line, never blocking, `skip=true` never re-asks, resolve only with the person's own answer) is G75's primer text — server-side filter, copy-only discipline. (7) Card: kind icon (never a monogram for non-entity kinds — made on the certain facts only; G97's refuted pfp explanation is NOT re-derived here and stays open there), line 1 question, line 2 cause (`From "<title>" · <harness> · <age>`), preview pane with the mention bolded from `mention_offsets`, `(Recommended)` row first, descriptions age-first then provenance (`you said this` / `Cicada inferred` / `Cicada's guess at 0.42`), `Other…`, footer `Not now — ask again in 7 days (l)`; copy rules enforced by a NEW `inbox_questions.validate_question` (Phase 1 code, ~30 lines — it does not exist today) in tests; keyboard via `QuestionSelection` (`1–4` pick, `⏎`, `o`, `l` = defer, `Esc` = collapse with NO write — a new enum case beside today's `pick/openOther/moveUp/moveDown/activate`, `c` conversation, `e` entity, `⌘[` back to the same card). (8) Silence: explicit later = defer + `defer_count` on the file; `defer_count ≥ 3` → 90-day auto-defer by the Stage 5.56 sweep as `cicada` (G85 shape); belief kinds (decay/conflict/divergence/normalization) silence-close under `inbox/silence_closed` (verdict `neutral`, reopen through the producers' merge-on-collision) — **clocked in Sleep cycles actually run (or the decay `decayed_through` watermark), never wall-clock** (TODO ruling 1: an engine outage is not user silence), and `silent_days` copy must never read as "why this decayed"; clarification/merge silence-close is owner question 3 (recommended: close too, 180 Sleep-counted days, traced and reopenable — recommended, not yet ruled). (9) Toast/tool result say what changed (`Archived alpha-project · 1 belief closed`), never the grade. **Rails.** Zero LLM on any read/generate/refresh/render path (G74/G80; a monkeypatched-providers test raises if touched); ledger ids/enums/counts/floats only — additive refs on the shipped `resolution` event (`surface`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`) plus new `ask` (ids only, per surfaced item) and `rule_written` (rule string) kinds in `FEEDBACK_KINDS` (R7) — never a behaviour input (settled #6; the proposal engine stays G113 slice 5, 💸 DECIDE under G78); user commits stay `Cicada-Author: user` and session-less pending **G116(b)**; the `"rodrigo"` observer literal at `inbox_service.py:813` becomes a `settings.observer_owner` (default `"user"`) rendered through one `observer_label`; no owner name in any template; every new field optional so older app builds decode. **Phases.** **1** (1–2 days, engine-free, no dependency — parallel to G113 slices 3–7 in its own worktree; the two touch disjoint functions of `inbox_service.py`): `inbox_context.py` (new: `resolve_cause`, `excerpt` with `mention_offsets`), schema fields, `_item_from_file` cause + decay synthesis + `recommended` via the shipped `_verdict`, `write_claim_nudges` persists `source_episode`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2, `validate_question` (new), Swift `InboxCause` decode + `QuestionSelection` cases + `.decay` routed through `QuestionView`, `observer_owner`. **Phase 1 DOES touch the resolve path** — it accepts `resolve`+`archive|keep` on decay as aliases of `archive`/`keep_active`, which must preserve the shipped R1 trigger labels that `test_inbox_resolution_provenance.py` asserts; `remind_later → _defer(7)` (R6) is G113 Task 5 and is pending on `dev` (`inbox_service.py:607` still writes `snooze_until`) — Phase 1 leans on it, does not own it. **2** (after G113 slices 3–4 and G106(i)): ask gate + MCP through `load_inbox` + `ask` event + handshake text (with G75), `never`/`defer:N` rules + `rules` component + Rules sheet, observer capsule + per-option excerpt + merge two-column preview (G103's first surface), `c`/`e`/`⌘[` navigation, response echo + `defer_count` persisted. **3** (after G116 and G75/G49): grouped cards (≤4 per entity, header chips, Now 12 / Later, `⌘⏎` accept-all-recommended excluding merges, `_inbox_counts` via `load_inbox`), Sleep-counted silence-close, the rule executor with the rule string in the manifest line, SessionStart injection replacing the copy-only per-session cap. → delivers **G97** (Phase 1, ahead of its wave position because every later phase stands on it) and the inbox half of **G113**; consumes **G60**, **G98**; first surface for **G103**; first caller of **G106**; evidence for **G108(b)**; carries **G75**'s inbox contract; gated by **G116** for rule execution and the session trailer. **Complaints answered by id:** **G114(3)** — the Telegram `/remind` reminder item is owned here as a `decay`-shaped `defer` with `surface: telegram` (G114 R4 parks it as a `capture_kind: reminder` note until then); **G110(b)** — `cause.conversation_id` on the wire plus `conversation_id?` on `cicada_check_nudges` is its approximation and `via_conversation_match` measures it, so do not reopen it; **G13** ("you had an open idea about X — still relevant?") rides the `decay` kind on a `concept`/`project` subject, no new kind; **G95 / G55 / G72** depend on the inbox staying one generic component — which is the argument for no new kinds (R4); **G100** stays where it is (asserted spans render as `span_kind: asserted` beside today's `derived`). **Owner, 2026-09-03 (evidence for Phase 1's card):** "the card has a chevron arrow at the end; I click on it but it doesn't do anything… the long list of URLs that doesn't end is not great" — two concrete defects the redesign must close first: a chevron that does nothing (either it opens the question or it is not drawn), and merge/clarification items over media that render every URL inline (collapse to a count + the top 3 with 'show all', never an unbounded list). The design study (judge panel memo, 2026-09-02) is already done — Phase 1 should start with these two. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — suggested outcome with a sentence of why (Phase 2, gated):** "some sort of agent that looks at the raw context of where the conflict or duplication is happening; if it passes a certain confidence threshold it politely gives a sentence of what it thinks the outcome should be — 'according to context I think it should be like this' — and the human confirms with a check mark or picks himself. Not every conflict gets a suggestion. Log these suggestions for later training." Design: this is the `(Recommended)` option plus a **rationale**, produced only when a judge over the item's cause spans (G118) is confident ≥ `inbox_suggest_min_confidence` (default 0.8) — below that the card shows options with no suggestion, exactly as today. The rationale is one sentence, written into the inbox item (bank, so it is provenance), attributed to the model, rendered under the recommended option as *Cicada thinks: …* with a one-key accept (⏎ on the recommended row) and the ordinary options beside it. Never auto-applied. **Training log:** the G113 `resolution` event already carries `recommended_key` and `picked_recommended`; add `suggestion_confidence` and `rationale_id` (ids/enums only — the sentence stays in the bank) so agreement-with-suggestion becomes a rate on `/consumption/feedback` and a labelled set for a future model. Runs in Stage 5 (the item is created there) or on demand for existing items, engine = the Sleep engine of the cycle, capped per cycle. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — research-resolvable conflicts (same Phase 2 agent, second tool):** "there are conflicts that can be solved by just looking up the repo — a conflict or clarification solved with research can be the outcome; maybe part of the Sleep cycle." Example seen on a project entity's `uses` predicate: `<architecture-concept>` vs `<database-x>` — the linked repo answers it in one grep (no such dependency). Design: the suggestion judge gets a bounded, read-only tool set drawn from what the entity already declares — `repos:` via `git_service` (file list, dependency manifests, grep; on demand, never cached), `sources:` URLs (G61, fetched under the G102 rules), and the cause spans — and its rationale must name what it checked (*"the repo's `pyproject.toml` has no `<database-x>` dependency; `<architecture-concept>` appears in `CLAUDE.md`"*); a rationale with no checked source is downgraded below the confidence gate. Runs where the item is created (Stage 5) or on demand from the card (*Research this*), capped per cycle, engine-free when only file reads are needed. The same item also shows a **G98** artefact: `uses` is a multi-valued predicate, so five true values were rendered as a conflict — Phase 1 must not ask for a winner on a predicate the schema marks multi-valued (cardinality from `_predicates.yaml`), only on single-valued ones. **Phase 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #48, `feat/inbox-phase1`):** `inbox_context.py` (cause, three tiers, `[ no source recorded ]`), additive wire fields (`entity_type`, `source_episode`, `claim_id`, `cause`, `extractor_confidence`/`_model`, `recommended_key`, `informational`; options `recommended` + `verdict`), decay synthesised at read with `archive` recommended, `recommended_key` == the unique `_verdict`-agreed key (never neither/both/merge/clarification; absent on entity-path conflicts), options served recommended-first, `remind_later → _defer(7)` (G113 R6 landed here), `_owner_observer(settings)`, ledger `recommended_key`/`picked_recommended`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2 + decay as a question on MCP, `validate_question`, the card (kind icon, question/cause lines, bolded excerpt, `QuestionView` for the two kinds that carry a question object — decay and conflict; clarification/merge keep their rows until Phase 2 — `1–4 ⏎ o l Esc`, real chevron, collapse past four lines, merge two-name preview, honest empty state), and the G98 informational rule. **Rulings made in the plan** (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-g115-inbox-phase1.md`): the informational dismiss grades `overruled` (the overruled belief is "these conflict"); clarification never carries a Recommended; decay's Recommended is `archive`; `cardinality` unions its two sources with `multi` winning rather than letting a stale bank map out-vote the seed; `_owner_observer`'s unset fallback stays the historical literal (not `user`) so an existing bank keeps one observer lineage — the choice moves to G117; the merge preview is two names + suggested type until G103. **Final review (3 fixes, same branch):** decay's `allow_other: false` is now honoured end to end — MCP's `Other / Later` line gates each half on its own flag (it was inviting free text an agent could not have honoured) and `_normalize_decay_request` 400s a `resolve`/`answer` carrying free text (it used to be appended to the entity body while the page stayed `decaying` and the item was deleted, inverting a “still relevant” answer); `recommended_key` synthesises decay's read-time options so `_emit_resolution` records the recommendation the card actually showed instead of `null`; and both MCP reader loops build ONE `InboxContext` instead of one per item (a per-item `episodes/`+`entities/` scan measured 425 ms for 40 items on a 2,000-episode bank, in the conversation path). Phases 2–3 unchanged. | 🔲 |

CLAUDE.md reserves `Cicada-Author: user` for manual/companion/media-save writes and `cicada` for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop (G85 precedent: the decay-only commit). Each option states its cost model in one line before it is chosen, not after — the G51 honesty rule ("subscription = flat $/mo, usage = real per-model $, local = free") applied at the moment of choosing rather than in a dashboard nobody opens. MCP registration status from `install.sh`, Telegram, an import drop, a bookmarks sync), *run your first Sleep* (`POST /sleep/trigger` with the live `sleep` SSE state, ending on the Graph tab); (2) an empty-state per tab that says what will fill it and offers the one action that does (Graph → import or capture; Inbox → "questions appear after a Sleep"; Feed → add a source; Activity → "nothing recorded yet"); (3) a **demo bank** the sheet can create in one click (`POST /banks` + a seeded synthetic set, reusing the 2026-09-02 demo-bank generator once it is checked in as a script — fictional people/projects only) so the graph is never blank on first launch and the person can try Sleep/inbox/decay before wiring their own life in; (4) the persisted `hasOnboarded` flag lives per bank in `UserDefaults`, and the sheet is re-openable from Settings. **Constraints:** no new dependency; no network beyond the endpoints named; the sheet reads the same `Store` snapshots as every other view (never blank, never a second source of truth); copy follows the Claude Code question rules (one action per step, recommended first, never a modal that blocks an existing user — it only appears when the active bank is empty); portability rails — no owner name, no author-machine path, the demo bank ships with placeholder names exactly as the test fixtures do. **Not in scope:** the paste-prompt install itself (G76), the strategic what-is-Cicada decision (G92 — this row assumes route (a), local-first), the onboarding *interview* that seeds memory from a conversation (G54; | G118 | **Full provenance and traceability of memory — every belief shows the raw span, the contributor's reasoning, and the prompt that caused the write (a better NotebookLM)** (Rodrigo 2026-09-02: "full provenance and traceability of memory, even for the UI where I can see a raw reference with the whole page or the highlighted part that is referenced… highlights of where the contributor got the information or what different parts of conversation (can also reference own reasoning) helped consolidate something in memory… also the user's prompts that made the model act like this, like traces. Like an even better version of how NotebookLM references something from the material you give it" — marked by the owner as central to the vision) | **What exists today, and where it stops:** a belief carries `source_episodes` (which conversation) and a commit carries `Cicada-Author:` / `Cicada-Session:` (which model, which conversation) — file-level and conversation-level provenance. It does not say *which sentence* (G100, unbuilt), *why the contributor concluded it* (Stage-1 output has no `evidence`/`reasoning` field that survives into the claim), or *what the person asked for* that led an agent to write (an MCP `cicada_write_claim` / `cicada_save_episode` call records the claim, not the turn that triggered it). `GET /entities/{id}/history` answers "when and by whom", never "from what". **The bar:** NotebookLM shows the cited passage inline; Cicada must do that *and* keep it bi-temporal (the span as it was when the belief formed), attributed (model, session, observer), and two-directional (from a span: everything it convinced; from a belief: every span, across conversations, links and notes). **Design — four layers, each additive to the existing files:** (1) **Spans, not copies.** A claim gains `evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind: user|assistant|reasoning|page}]` — byte offsets into the stored episode/page text, never a quoted copy (privacy: the bank already holds the text; the ledger stays ids-only). Stage-1 extraction returns the exact substring it relied on; the resolver validates it exists in the episode and stores offsets (an unverifiable span is recorded as `kind: reasoning` with no offsets, never faked). Absorbs **G100**. (2) **Reasoning as a citable source.** The contributor's own justification (the model's `why` for a merge, a conflict pick, a decay archive, a skill) is stored as a short `rationale` on the claim/nudge with the model id — the model citing itself is a first-class evidence kind, shown distinctly from what the person said. (3) **Trigger traces.** Every agent write records the *turn that caused it*: for MCP writes, the calling tool's `context` (the user's last message excerpt as a span in the episode the MCP seam already captures — never read from `~/.claude` transcripts, G48 rail) and the tool name; for Sleep writes, the cycle id and stage. Surfaced as `trigger: {kind: user_prompt|agent_decision|sleep_stage, span?, tool?, cycle?}`. Consumes **G113**'s ledger shape (ids/enums) and **G105** (deterministic capture is what makes 'the turn that caused it' always exist). (4) **The viewer.** Entity page → claim → evidence chips → a raw-source pane that opens the whole episode/page with the cited spans highlighted and the rest dimmed, scrollable, with the other beliefs that cite the same passage listed alongside; from `cicada_ask` answers, every citation is a span, not a page (**G93**'s output half); from an inbox card, the cause span (**G97**/**G115**). Keyboard: ⌘[ back to the belief. **Rails:** engine-free read path (offsets → slices, ≤ a few ms); ETag ship-together (episodes join the entity/inbox ETags where the span text is served); transcripts under `~/.claude` are never read — if the MCP seam did not capture the turn, the trace says so; spans go stale when an episode is rewritten — store the episode's content hash beside the offsets and mark `stale` on mismatch rather than mis-highlight; nothing here is auto-applied. **Ordering:** span capture at extraction (Stage-1 + resolver) first, because every later layer reads it; then the viewer; then triggers; then reasoning. → absorbs **G100**; consumes **G105**, **G113**, **G48**; feeds **G93** (cited answers), **G97**/**G115** (cause on cards), **G106** (conversation ↔ entity browser is the same pane), **G101** (the evidence layer is what makes raw conversations worth keeping), **G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` becomes real citations). APPLY, L (four slices, each shippable). **Slice 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #44, `feat/provenance-spans`):** `Claim.evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind, hash}]` (offsets into the parsed body, ```claims fence excluded for a page; `sha256[:12]` → `stale` on mismatch; kinds `user|assistant|page|reasoning`, speaker by the last `<role>:` marker, no marker = user); one engine-free module `api/services/evidence.py` (exact → whitespace → case-insensitive, never fuzzy; unlocatable = `reasoning`, claim still written); Stage-1 `evidence_quote` verified in `extract` against the chunked body with the chunk as window; `cicada_write_claim`/`write_claim` take `evidence: [{episode, quote}]`; link recon `page` spans on the media entity; Telegram `saved-because` cites its section; `_reinforce` merges spans; `evidence` on `/claims`, `/timeline`, `/transclude`; `GET /episodes/{id}/span`. Legacy claims: no evidence, no backfill, `to_dict` omits the empty key. **Open:** slice 2 viewer (entity → claim → chip → raw pane, Swift `Evidence` model), slice 3 trigger traces, slice 4 rationale, G100's derived-span class, `describes` claims on link enrichment (a whole-section span — trivial once the viewer wants it). | 🛠️ slice 1 ✅ (PR #44); slices 2–4 open |

| G120 | **Attention frequency — track who and what you keep coming back to (channels, accounts, authors, people) and let recurrence shape the graph** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03: "in social media like YouTube or Twitter there's always a certain number of channels or people who I love to see their tweets and stay informed. Do you think frequency in mentions also has to influence something in the memory graph, or at least should we track it?") | **What exists:** frequency already drives one thing — decay (`conflict_resolver`: confidence drops proportional to how often an entity USED to be referenced, CLAUDE.md Temporal Decay), and the promotion rule counts conversations (2+ mentions across conversations). Nothing counts **sources**: a saved video carries `media.url` and an OG title, a tweet carries an author handle in its URL, a newsletter carries a sender — but the *channel/account/author behind the item* is never made an entity, so 40 saves from one YouTube channel are 40 unrelated media pages and the person the owner follows most is invisible to the graph and to retrieval. **Design (three layers, all engine-free):** (1) **Source attribution at ingest** — `media_ingestor` derives a `source_ref` `{platform, kind: channel|account|author|domain, id, display}` from the URL/metadata it already has (YouTube channel from the page's OG/`author_name`, X/Twitter handle from the path, Substack/newsletter sender from the feed row, podcast show, GitHub org, plain domain as the fallback); stored on the media episode + entity frontmatter, no fetch. (2) **Recurrence ledger + promotion** — a per-bank `_attention.yaml` (or a derived index — decide; must be rebuildable from the pages, never a second source of truth) counting saves/mentions per source_ref per week; when a source crosses the existing promotion threshold (≥ 2 saves across ≥ 2 weeks, or ≥ 3 saves) Sleep creates a `person`/`company`/`concept` entity for the channel/author with a `follows` claim (`observer` = user-derived, `source_trust: observed`, confidence from recurrence), `evergreen` never — it decays like any belief so a channel you stop watching fades honestly (the counter-signal is the whole point). Media pages get an `about`/`from` edge to it. (3) **Surface** — Feed gets a "You keep coming back to" strip (top sources by recent recurrence, with the count and last-seen date), the entity page shows the recurrence sparkline, `cicada_recall` weights a source's items by recurrence, and G111's "what landed today that matters" uses it as the ranking signal. **Rails:** counts only — no engagement scraping, no fetching follower lists, nothing behind auth (G102 ToS rail); ids/enums in any ledger (G113); a recurrence claim is `observed`, never `user_stated`, and the inbox may ask "still following <source>?" through the ordinary decay path rather than a new kind; portability — platform parsers are the same adapter shape as `connectors/`. **Not the same as:** G86 (feed dedup), G94 (life-data aggregates — that is samples→aggregates from wearables; this is recurrence over what you save and read), G111 (newsletters — a consumer of this signal). → feeds **G111**, **G93** (retrieval weight), **G118** (a `follows` claim cites the saves that earned it), **G81** (a recurring author is an identity anchor); consumes **G47**/**G71** (saved-content importers already carry the URLs). APPLY, M | 🔲 |
| G121 | **World facts vs personal facts — a place (or company, tool) page must be anchored on why it matters to the person; encyclopedia facts are a dated cache an agent re-verifies, never truth** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03, looking at a location page whose summary and Key Facts were metro lines and court names: "what if something changes in that location and an agent in the future references the memory instead of researching the place? Should information about a place be just why it's relevant to the user?") | **Diagnosis:** the extractor fills a `location`/`company`/`tool` page with whatever the model knows — none of it observed in an episode — and writes it with the same `source_trust: agent_extracted` and confidence as things the person actually said. Two failures follow: the fact goes stale silently (a renamed venue, a closed line), and an agent reading the page cannot tell a remembered fact from a looked-up one, so it may act on the cache instead of checking. `claims.py:71` knows `user_stated|agent_extracted|agent_reflected|external`; nothing marks *model knowledge*. **Ruling to adopt:** a memory is about the person's experience; the world is not Cicada's to remember. Every page has two tiers: (1) **personal claims** — the relation to the person (visited, plans, works-with, saved-because, decided) with the episode span (G118); authoritative. (2) **world facts** — convenience context; allowed only when tagged `source_trust: model_knowledge` (new value, decay multiplier ≥ `external`), `decay_class: volatile`, dated by `recorded_at`, and paired where possible with a `sources:` pointer (G61) to where the current truth lives (the venue's site, a docs URL). **Changes:** (a) the Stage-1 prompt asks for the relation first and marks anything not grounded in the text as model knowledge — a page whose only claims are world facts is a promotion-rule violation (nothing personal was observed) and stays a candidate; (b) `entity_extractor`/`claim_pipeline` accept the new trust value; existing pages get a one-shot backfill that re-tags claims without a source span as `model_knowledge` (dry-run count first on the live bank — expect most `location` Key Facts to flip); (c) the entity card renders the two tiers apart — "Why it's in your memory" above, "Context (as of <date>, from the model's knowledge — verify before relying on it)" below, with the sources link; (d) `cicada_recall` / `cicada_ask` label world facts as cached-on-<date> in their output, and the G75 handshake carries the rule in one sentence: *personal facts are authoritative; world facts are a cache — check before acting*; (e) the inbox never asks a decay question about a world fact (it simply fades). **Rails:** no fetching to refresh world facts in this row (that is G102's tail, gated); ledger ids only; the backfill's commit is `Cicada-Author: cicada` with the exact pages named. → depends on **G118** (a span is what separates the tiers), **G61** (sources), **G66** (decay classes); feeds **G75** (handshake), **G93**/**G52** (answer labelling), **G53** (state dictionary carries only personal facts).
